Solutions with clustering concentration layers to the Ambrosetti-Prodi type problem Thanks: † Q. Ren is supported by Inner Mongolia Autonomous Region Natural Science Foundation(no. 2024LHMS01001) and NSF of China(no. 12001298)
Qiang Ren †

Abstract
. We consider the following Ambrosetti-Prodi type problem { − div ( A ( x ) ∇ u ) = | u | p − t 𝚿 ( x ) , in Ω , u = 0 , on ∂ Ω , \left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Div(A(x)\nabla u)=|u|^{p}-t\mathbf{\Psi}(x),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\ u=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right. (0.1) where Ω ⊂ ℝ 2 \Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2} , t > 0 t>0 , p > 3 p>3 and 𝚿 \mathbf{\Psi} is an eigenfunction corresponding to the first eigenvalue of the following operator 𝔏 ( u ) = − div ( A ( x ) ∇ u ) . \mathfrak{L}(u)=-\Div(A(x)\nabla u). Moreover, A ⁡ ( x ) = { A i ​ j ​ ( x ) } 2 × 2 A(x)=\{A_{ij}(x)\}_{2\times 2} is a symmetric positive defined matrix function. Let Γ ⊂ Ω \Gamma\subset\Omega be a closed curve and also a non-degenerate critical point of the functional 𝒦 ⁡ ( Γ ) = ∫ Γ 𝚿 p + 3 2 ​ p ​ 𝑑 v ​ o ​ l 𝔤 , \mathcal{K}(\Gamma)=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p+3}{2p}}dvol_{\mathfrak{g}}, where 𝔤 ⁡ ( X , Y ) = ⟨ A ∗ ​ X , Y ⟩ \mathfrak{g}(X,Y)=\langle A^{*}X,Y\rangle is a Riemannian metric on ℝ 2 \mathbb{R}^{2} and A ∗ A^{*} is the adjoint matrix for A A . We prove that there exists a sequence of t = t l → + ∞ t=t_{l}\to+\infty such that ( 0.1 ) has solutions u t l u_{t_{l}} with clustering concentration layers directed along Γ \Gamma .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35B25, 35B40

1.
Introduction

The Ambrosetti-Prodi problem is of the following form:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Delta u=\zeta(u)-t\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}(x),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
u=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(1.1)

where
t>0
,
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{N}}
is a smooth bounded domain,
\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}
is the an eigenfunction of
-\Delta
subject to Dirichlet boundary condition corresponding to the first eigenvalue, the function
\zeta(t)
satisfies

-\infty\leq\mu=\lim_{t\to+\infty}\frac{\zeta(t)}{t}>\lim_{t\to+\infty}\frac{\zeta(t)}{t}=\nu\leq+\infty,

and the interval
(\mu,\nu)
contains some eigenvalues of
-\Delta
subject to Dirichlet boundary condition.

Problem (
1.1
) was first studied by Ambrosetti and Prodi
[
3
]
. It was widely discussed in 1980’s( see
[
16
,
17
,
26
,
27
,
28
,
38
,
39
,
40
,
37
]
for example). The main results in these literature are that if
g(t)
grows subcritical at infinity, (
1.1
) has at least two solutions: one is local minimizer of the Euler-Lagrange functional, the other is the mountain-pass solution. Breuer, McKenna and Plum
[
7
]
considered the case that
\zeta(t)=t^{2}
and
\Omega
is a unit square in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Using a computer assisted proof, they showed that (
1.1
) has at least 4 solutions. By comparing Morse index of mountain pass solution in different spaces, de Figueiredo, Srikanth and Santra
[
18
]
found a non-radial solution of (
1.1
) under the condition
\Omega
is a unit ball and
\zeta(t)=t^{2}
.

However, in the case of
\zeta(t)=|t|^{p}
, where
1<p<\frac{\mathcal{N}+2}{\mathcal{N}-2}
for
\mathcal{N}\geq 3
and
p>1
for
\mathcal{N}=2
, (
1.1
) becomes the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Delta u=|u|^{p}-t\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}(x),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
u=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$}.\end{array}\right.

(1.2)

Dancer and Yan
[
14
]
constructed arbitrary many peak solutions of (
1.2
) for
t>0
large enough. It shows that Lazer-McKenna conjecture
[
26
]
holds in this case. Dancer and Yan
[
14
]
also proved that the mountain pass solution of (
1.1
) has a sharp peak near the boundary for
t
large enough. As a conclusion, (
1.1
) has solutions concentrating at some points in
\Omega
or the ones on
\partial\Omega
as
t\to\infty
. These results have been extended to different kind of nonlinearities( see
[
15
,
11
,
21
,
31
,
30
,
35
,
43
]
for instance and references therein).

However, these results concern only point concentrating solutions. Based on some numerical evidence, Hollman and McKenna
[
24
]
asked (
1.2
) that whether there exist other types of concentrations for the solutions to (
1.2
) as
t\to+\infty
. For this problem, Manna and Santra
[
34
]
considered (
1.2
) under the condition
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
p>2
. They proved that (
1.2
) has a family of solutions clustering along a closed curve
\Gamma\subset\Omega
, where
\Gamma
is a non-degenerate critical point of the functional

\mathcal{K}_{0}(\Gamma)=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}^{\frac{p+3}{2p}}(x)dvol.

(1.3)

Later, this result was extended to high dimensional case by Khemiri, Mahmoudi and Messaoudi
[
25
]
. Under the condition that
\mathcal{N}\geq 3
and
\Omega
contain a
k
-dimensional compact submanifold
\Gamma
, which is a non-degenerate critical point of the functional

\mathcal{K}_{1}(\Gamma)=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Psi}_{0}^{\left(1-\frac{1}{p}\right)\left(\frac{p+1}{p-1}-\frac{n-k}{2}\right)}(x)dvol,

They proved there exists a sequence
t=t_{j}\to\infty
, and solutions
u_{t_{j}}
to (
1.2
), which have concentration layers concentrating near
\Gamma
.

Baraket
et. al.

[
5
]
considered the following Neumann problem:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Delta u=|u|^{p}-t\psi(x),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
\frac{\partial u}{\partial\mathbf{n}}=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(1.4)

where
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{N}}
is a smooth bound domain and
\mathbf{n}
is the unit outward normal vector of
\partial\Omega
.
Under the condition
\mathcal{N}=2
, they proved that (
1.4
) has a solution
u_{t}
concentrating along a curve
\Gamma\subset\bar{\Omega}
with
t>0
large enough. The curve
\Gamma
intersects
\partial\Omega
with right angle and divides
\Omega
into two part. Moreover
\Gamma
is a non-degenerate critical point of the functional
\mathcal{K}_{2}(\Gamma)=\int_{\Gamma}\psi^{\frac{p+3}{2p}}(x)dvol
. However, under the condition that
\psi\equiv 1
and
\mathcal{N}\geq 2
, Bendahou, Khemiri and Mahmoudi
[
6
]
constructed a family of new solutions to (
1.4
), which has large number of spikes concentrating along an interior straight line in
\Omega
for
t\to+\infty
. Using the similar method, Ao, Fu and Liu
[
4
]
constructed a similar type of solutions which concentrate along a segment of boundary
\partial\Omega
in the two dimensional case.

From these results, we recognize that the high dimensional concentration behaviours of Ambrosetti-Prodi type problem is similar to that of Nonlinear Schrödinger equation:

-\varepsilon^{2}\Delta u+V(y)u=u^{p},\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{N}}.

(1.5)

In 2003, Ambrosetti, Malchiodi and Ni
[
2
]
raised the following conjecture:

Conjecture 1.1
.

Let
\Gamma
be a
k
-dimensional submanifold in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{N}}
and a nondegenerate critical point of the following functional

\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)=\int_{\Gamma}V^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathcal{N}-k)}dvol,

where
1<p<\frac{n+2-k}{n-2+k}
for
\mathcal{N}\geq 3
and
p>1
for
\mathcal{N}=2
.
Then there exists a family of solutions to (
1.5
) concentrating along
\Gamma
at least for a subsequence
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{j}\to 0
.

del Pino, Kowalczyk and Wei
[
19
]
first showed that this conjecture holds in the case of
\mathcal{N}=2
and
k=1
. Wang, Wei and Yang
[
41
]
proved that this conjecture in the case of
\mathcal{N}\geq 3
and
k=\mathcal{N}-1
. And Mahmoudi, Sanchez and Yao
[
33
]
proved this conjecture is valid for all cases.

However, the following more general Neumann version of (
1.5
) also has some solutions concentrating on high dimensional set:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\varepsilon^{2}\Div(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}(y)}u)-V(y)u+u^{p}=0,\qquad u>0,&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}(y)}u\cdot\mathbf{n}=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$,}\end{array}\right.

(1.6)

where
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a smooth bounded domain,
\mathbf{n}
is the unit outward normal of
\partial\Omega
,

\mathfrak{a}(y)=(\mathfrak{a}_{1}(y),\mathfrak{a}_{2}(y),\cdots,\mathfrak{a}_{N}(y))

and

\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}(y)}u=(\mathfrak{a}_{1}(y)u_{y_{1}},\mathfrak{a}_{2}(y)u_{y_{2}},\cdots,\mathfrak{a}_{N}(y)u_{y_{N}}).

In the case of
\mathfrak{a}\equiv 1
and
V(y)\equiv 1
, Wei and Yang
[
44
]
constructed a sequence of solutions concentrating near a segment
\Gamma_{2}
in
\Omega
. The segment intersects
\partial\Omega
with right angle and separates
\Omega
into two parts. Unlike the solutions constructed in
[
19
,
41
,
33
]
, the solutions constructed in
[
44
]
have multiple concentration layers.
Wei, Xu and Yang
[
45
]
considered the case that
\mathfrak{a}(y)\equiv 1
. Let
\Gamma\subset\bar{\Omega}
be a curve intersecting
\partial\Omega
with right angle and separating
\Omega
into two parts. Provided
\Gamma
is a nondegenerate critical point of
\int_{\Gamma}V^{\frac{p+1}{p-1}-\frac{1}{2}}
, they constructed a sequence of solutions to (
1.6
) clustering along
\Gamma
. Recently, Wei and Yang
[
46
]
extended the result in
[
44
]
into the general case. They constructed a sequence of solutions to (
1.6
) with multiple
concentration layers which concentrate near a closed curve
\Gamma_{0}\subset\Omega
or a curve
\Gamma_{1}
intersecting
\partial\Omega
with right angle and separating
\Omega
into two parts. Moreover,
\Gamma_{0}
and
\Gamma_{1}
are all the nondegenerate geodesics embedded into the Riemannian manifold
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with the metric
V^{\frac{2(p+1)}{p-1}-1}[\mathfrak{a}_{2}(y)dy_{1}^{2}+\mathfrak{a}_{1}(y)dy_{2}^{2}]
.

Inspired by
[
46
,
44
]
, we consider the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Div(A(x)\nabla u)=|u|^{p}-t\mathbf{\Psi}(x),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
u=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(1.7)

where
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a smooth bounded domain,
t>0
is a constant, and
\mathbf{\Psi}(x)
is an eigenfunction corresponding to the first Dirichlet eigenvalue of the operator
\mathfrak{L}(u)=-\Div(A(x)\nabla u)
on
\Omega
.
Moreover,
A(x)=\{A_{ij}(x)\}_{2\times 2}
is a symmetric positive defined matrix function, satisfying

\lambda|\alpha|^{2}\leq\langle A(x)\alpha,\alpha\rangle\leq\Lambda|\alpha|^{2},\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad x,\alpha\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(1.8)

We notice
\Div(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}(y)}u)
is a special form of
\Div(A(x)\nabla u)
. Based on previous work, we suspect whether (
1.7
) has a similar solution to that constructed in
[
46
,
44
]
.

Our motivation for writing this paper is twofold. First, we plan to construct solutions to (
1.7
) with multiple concentration layers. Second, we explore the influence of the matrix
A(y)
to the high dimensional concentration behaviours of the solutions to (
1.7
).

Let
\varepsilon^{2}=t^{-(p-1)/p}
. It is easy to get that
u
is a solution of (
1.7
) if and only if
t^{-\frac{1}{p}}u
is the solution of the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla u)=|u|^{p}-\mathbf{\Psi}(x),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
u=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(1.9)

Then we get the following theorem.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
p>3
.
Assume that
\Gamma
is a simple closed smooth curve with unit length in
\Omega
, and it is also a non-degenerate critical point of the functional

\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)=\int_{\Gamma}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p+3}{2p}}dvol_{\mathfrak{g}},

(1.10)

where
\mathfrak{g}(X,Y)=\langle A^{*}X,Y\rangle
is a Riemannian metric on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
A^{*}
is the adjoint matrix for
A
. We also assume that the following inequality holds on
\Gamma
:

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{1-p}\left[2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}a_{22}+\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}b_{11}+b_{22}-a_{33}+\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}+a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}-\beta^{-2}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}\right)\right]>0,

where
a_{ij}
’s and
b_{ij}
’s is defined in Lemma
2.2
,
\alpha
and
\beta
are defined in (
3.3
), and
\mathbf{q}
is defined in (
2.2
).
Then for each integer
N>0
, there exists a sequence of
\varepsilon
, i.e.,
\{\varepsilon_{l}\}
converging to
0
such that (
1.9
) has a positive solutions
u_{\varepsilon_{l}}
with exactly
N
concentration layers at mutual distance
O(\varepsilon_{l}|\log\varepsilon_{l}|)
, the center of mass of
N
concentration layers collapses to
\Gamma
at speed
\varepsilon_{l}^{1+\mu}
for small positive constant
\mu\in(0,1/2)
. More precisely
u_{\varepsilon_{l}}
has the form

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon_{l}}(y_{1},y_{2})\approx-\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}(y_{1},y_{2})+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}(\gamma(\theta))\sum_{k=1}^{N}w\left(\left[\frac{\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle)}{\langle A^{*}n,n\rangle}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{t-\varepsilon_{l}f_{k}}{\varepsilon_{l}}\right),

where
\gamma
is a natural parametrization of
\Gamma
,
n
is the unit vector defined in (
2.1
) and
w
is the unique solution of the following problem

-w^{\prime\prime}=|1-w|^{p}-1,\qquad w>0\quad\mathrm{in}\quad\mathbb{R},\qquad w^{\prime}(0)=w(\pm\infty).

(1.11)

In the expression above, the functions
f_{j}
’s satisfy

\|f_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C|\log\varepsilon_{l}|^{2},\qquad\sum_{j=1}^{N}f_{j}=O(\frac{1}{|\log\varepsilon_{l}|^{\frac{3}{2}}}),

\min_{1\leq j\leq N}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})\approx\frac{2}{\sqrt{p}}|\log\varepsilon_{l}|\left[\frac{\langle A^{*}n,n\rangle}{\Psi^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle)}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

and solves the Jacobi-Toda system for
j=1,2,\cdots,N

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\alpha^{1-p}\beta\left\{-a_{11}f_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\left[a_{22}-b_{11}-a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)\right]f_{j}^{\prime}+\left[a_{22}(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime})+b_{22}-a_{33}\right]f_{j}\right.

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}\right]f_{j}

\displaystyle C_{0}p\alpha_{p}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]\approx 0.

Remark 1.3
.

The condition that
\Gamma
is a non-degenerate critical point of the functional
\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)
is equivalent to the condition that
\Gamma
is a non-degenerate geodesic embedded into the Riemannian manifold
(\mathbb{R}^{2},\tilde{\mathfrak{g}})
, where
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}(y)=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p+3}{p}}[A_{22}dy_{1}^{2}-2A_{12}(y)dy_{1}dy_{2}+A_{11}dy_{2}^{2}]
.

We will use the infinite dimensional reduction method developed in
[
19
,
20
,
46
]
to prove Theorem
1.2
. Infinite dimensional reduction method is used to find solutions to elliptic partial differential equations concentrating on high dimensional sets. To construct a solution to (
1.9
), we first investigate the negative solution of (
1.9
) and its property in Section
B
. However, we need to overcome the difficulty that
-\Div(A(x)\nabla u)
is not a symmetric operator( see Lemma
B.2
for detail). To get a local approximate solution to (
1.9
), we need to expand the operator
\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla v)
in a proper way. Wei and Yang
[
46
]
developed a method to expand
\Div(\nabla_{\mathfrak{a}}(y)u)
. However we find a easier way to expand this operator. Similar to
[
46
]
, to construct local approximate solution with
N
concentration layers, we need a fine asymptotic estimate of the function
w
. But
w
does not have the explicit expression(comparing
[
46
, (3.1)]
). To overcome this difficulty, we use the method in
[
22
]
to get the asymptotic estimate of the function
w
in the Section
A
.

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we set up a new modified Fermi coordinate in some neighborhood of
\Gamma
and write down a local form of the operator
\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla u)
in the stretched modified Fermi coordinate. In Section
3
, we find a local approximate solution to (
1.9
). Then we conduct the gluing procedure in Section
4
and the outer problem is solved. To solve the inner problem, we construct linear and nonlinear theory in Section
5
and Section
6
, respectively. From some involved calculation in Section
C
, we reduce the problem into some partial differential equations in Section
7
. At last, Theorem
1.2
is proven in Section
8
.

2.
geometric description

Let
\gamma:[0,1]\to\Gamma\subset\Omega
be a natural parametrization of
\Gamma
and
\nu
be the outward unit normal vector of
\Gamma
. The following Frenet formula holds:

\gamma^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=k(\theta)\nu(\theta),\qquad\nu^{\prime}(\theta)=-k(\theta)\gamma^{\prime}(\theta),

where
k(\theta)
is the curvature of
\Gamma
. For
\delta>0
small enough, the
\delta
-neighborhood of
\Gamma
is parameterized by

y=\gamma(\theta)+t\nu(\theta),\quad\mathrm{where}\quad t\in[0,1],\quad t\in(-\delta,\delta).

In order to construct solutions to (
1.7
) near
\Gamma
, we need the modify the Fermi coordinate above. Define the following unit vector on
\Gamma
by

n(\theta)=\frac{A(\gamma(\theta))\nu(\theta)}{|A(\gamma(\theta))\nu(\theta)|}.

(2.1)

Then the following map is a local diffeomorphism:

\Phi^{0}(\theta,t)=\gamma(\theta)+tn(\theta),\quad\mathrm{where}\quad\theta\in[0,1],\quad t\in(-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}),

and
\delta_{0}>0
is a constant small enough.
Under this local coordinate, the components of the standard Riemanian metric of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
under this coordinate is represented by

\tilde{g}_{11}(\theta,t)=\left\langle\frac{\partial\Phi^{0}}{\partial\theta},\frac{\partial\Phi^{0}}{\partial\theta}\right\rangle=1+2t\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\theta),n^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle+t^{2}\langle n^{\prime}(\theta),n^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle,

\tilde{g}_{12}(\theta,t)=\tilde{g}_{21}(\theta,t)=\left\langle\frac{\partial\Phi^{0}}{\partial\theta},\frac{\partial\Phi^{0}}{\partial t}\right\rangle=\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\theta),n(\theta)\rangle

and

\tilde{g}_{22}(\theta,t)=\left\langle\frac{\partial\Phi^{0}}{\partial t},\frac{\partial\Phi^{0}}{\partial t}\right\rangle=1.

Hence

\det\tilde{g}=1-\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\theta),n(\theta)\rangle^{2}+2t\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\theta),n^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle+t^{2}\langle n^{\prime}(\theta),n^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle.

From direct computation, we get

\tilde{g}^{11}(\theta,t)=\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-t\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}+t^{2}\left[\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{3}}-\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{2}}\right]+O(t^{3}),

\tilde{g}^{12}(\theta,t)=\tilde{g}^{21}(\theta,t)=-\frac{\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+t\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}+t^{2}\left[\frac{\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}-\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{3}}\right]+O(t^{3})

and

\tilde{g}^{22}(\theta,t)=\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-t\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}+t^{2}\left[\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{3}}-\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{2}}\right]+O(t^{3}).

For notation simplicity, we denote

A(\theta,t)=A(\gamma(\theta)+tn(\theta)),\qquad{A^{*}}(\theta,t)={A^{*}}(\gamma(\theta)+tn(\theta))

and

\mathbf{q}(\theta,t)=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}(\gamma(\theta)+tn(\theta)).

(2.2)

Recall the functional
\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)
is defined in (
1.10
). Then we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 2.1
.

If the simple closed curve
\Gamma\subset\Omega
is also a non-degenerate critical point of the functional
\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)
, then we have:

1.

It holds that

\frac{p+3}{2}\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\mathbf{q}_{t}=-\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)\mathbf{q}\quad\mathrm{on}\quad\Gamma.

(2.3)

2.

The following equation has only trivial solution

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}h^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}+\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)h

\displaystyle-\left[\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right]_{\theta}h-\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)^{2}h

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{p+3}{2}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\mathbf{q}_{tt}-\frac{(p+3)(p+5)}{4}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{t})^{2}\right]\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}h=0.

(2.4)

Proof.

Given any function
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z})
, we consider the following closed curves:

\Gamma_{h}:\quad\gamma_{h}(\theta)=\gamma(\theta)+h(\theta)n(\theta).

It is apparent that
\Gamma_{0}=\Gamma
. The functional
J(h):=\mathcal{K}(\Gamma_{h})
is of the following form:

J(h)=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p+3}{2p}}(\gamma_{h}(\theta))\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}(\gamma_{h}(\theta))\gamma_{h}^{\prime}(\theta),\gamma_{h}^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle}d\theta=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}(\theta,h(\theta))\sqrt{W(\theta,h(\theta))}d\theta,

(2.5)

where
W(\theta,h(\theta)):=\langle{A^{*}}(\gamma_{h}(\theta))\gamma_{h}^{\prime}(\theta),\gamma_{h}^{\prime}(\theta)\rangle
. Hence
0
is a non-degenerate critical point of
J(h)
. It holds that

J^{\prime}(0)h=0,\qquad\forall h\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}).

A direct computation yields that

\displaystyle W(\theta,h(\theta))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+h(\theta)\left[2\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]+2h(\theta)h^{\prime}(\theta)\left[\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]

(2.6)

\displaystyle+(h^{\prime}(\theta))^{2}\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle+(h(\theta))^{2}\left[\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+O(|h|^{3})+O(|h^{\prime}||h|^{2})+O(|h||h^{\prime}|^{2}),

where we use the fact that
\langle A^{*}\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle=\frac{\det A}{|A\nu|}\langle\gamma^{\prime},\nu\rangle=0
on
\Gamma
.
Then we get

J^{\prime}(0)h=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left[\frac{p+3}{2}\mathbf{q}_{t}\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\mathbf{q}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right]hd\theta.

Hence (
2.3
) holds. From (
2.3
), (
2.6
) and direct computation,

\displaystyle J^{\prime\prime}(0)[h,h]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\left[\frac{(p+3)(p+1)}{4}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{t})^{2}+\frac{p+3}{2}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\mathbf{q}_{tt}\right]\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}h^{2}d\theta

\displaystyle+\frac{p+3}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+1}{2}}\mathbf{q}_{t}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left[2\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]h^{2}d\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left[\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)h^{2}+\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle(h^{\prime})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\left(\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)hh^{\prime}\right]d\theta-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left[\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]^{2}h^{2}d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}(h^{\prime})^{2}d\theta+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)h^{2}d\theta

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}\left[\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right]_{\theta}h^{2}d\theta-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}}{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle^{\frac{3}{2}}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)^{2}h^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\left[\frac{p+3}{2}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p+3}{2}}\mathbf{q}_{tt}-\frac{(p+3)(p+5)}{4}\mathbf{q}^{\frac{p-1}{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{t})^{2}\right]\sqrt{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}h^{2}.

Since
0
is a nondegenerate critical point of
J(h)
, we get (
2
) has only trivial solutions.
∎

In some neighborhood of
\Gamma_{\varepsilon}=\Gamma/\varepsilon
, we define the stretched modified Fermi coordinate by

\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z,s)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Phi^{0}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\left(\gamma(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon sn(\varepsilon z)\right),\quad\mathrm{where}\quad z\in[0,1/\varepsilon],\quad s\in(-\delta_{0}/\varepsilon,\delta_{0}/\varepsilon).

Under this coordinate, the components of the Riemanian metric are

g_{11}(z,s)=\left\langle\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle=1+2\varepsilon s\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\varepsilon z),n^{\prime}(\varepsilon z)\rangle+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\langle n^{\prime}(\varepsilon z),n^{\prime}(\varepsilon z)\rangle,

g_{12}(z,s)=g_{21}(z,s)=\left\langle\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle=\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\varepsilon z),n(\varepsilon z)\rangle

and

g_{22}(z,s)=\left\langle\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle=1.

Then

\det g=1-\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\varepsilon z),n(\varepsilon z)\rangle^{2}+2\varepsilon s\langle\gamma^{\prime}(\varepsilon z),n^{\prime}(\varepsilon z)\rangle+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\langle n^{\prime}(\varepsilon z),n^{\prime}(\varepsilon z)\rangle.

The inverse coefficients
(g^{ij})_{2\times 2}
have the following expansion

g^{11}(z,s)=\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\varepsilon s\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{3}}-\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{2}}\right]+O(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}),

\displaystyle g^{12}(z,s)=g^{21}(z,s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+\varepsilon s\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[\frac{\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}-\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{3}}\right]+O(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3})

and

g^{22}(z,s)=\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\varepsilon s\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{3}}-\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}{({1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle}^{2})^{2}}\right]+O(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}).

Now we expand the operator
\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla v)
under the local coordinate
(z,s)
.

Lemma 2.2
.

Under the stretched modified Fermi coordinate defined by
\Phi_{\varepsilon}(z,s)
, we get

\displaystyle\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{11}(\varepsilon z)v_{zz}+2\varepsilon sa_{22}(\varepsilon z)v_{sz}+\left(a_{31}(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon sa_{32}(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}a_{33}(\varepsilon z)\right)v_{ss}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon b_{11}(\varepsilon z)v_{z}+\left(\varepsilon b_{21}(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon^{2}sb_{22}(\varepsilon z)\right)v_{s}+B_{0}(v),

where

a_{11}(\theta)=\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}},\qquad a_{22}(\theta)=-\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\frac{\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}},

a_{31}(\theta)=\frac{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}},\qquad a_{32}=\frac{\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+\frac{2\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle a_{33}(\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}}\left(\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{3}}-\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle,

b_{11}(\theta)=\left[\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}\right]_{\theta}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}\right)_{\theta}\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle-\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\frac{\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}},

b_{21}(\theta)=\frac{\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}a_{31}+a_{32},

b_{22}(\theta)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})_{\theta}}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}a_{22}+\left[\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2})^{2}}\right]a_{31}+\frac{\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}a_{32}+\partial_{\theta}a_{22}+2a_{33}

and

B_{0}(v)=\varepsilon a_{12}(\varepsilon z,s)v_{zz}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{23}(\varepsilon z.s)v_{zs}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{33}(\varepsilon z,s)v_{ss}+\varepsilon^{2}b_{12}(\varepsilon z,s)v_{z}+\varepsilon^{3}b_{23}(\varepsilon z,s)v_{s}.

In the expression above, funtions
a_{12}
,
a_{23}
,
a_{33}
,
b_{12}
and
b_{23}
are smooth functions satisfying the following estimate

|a_{12}(\varepsilon z,s)|\leq C(1+|s|),\quad|a_{23}(\varepsilon z,s)|\leq C(1+|s|^{2}),\quad|a_{33}(\varepsilon z,s)|\leq C(1+|s|^{3}),

and

|b_{12}(\varepsilon z,s)|\leq C(1+|s|),\quad|b_{23}(\varepsilon z,s)|\leq C(1+|s|^{2}).

Proof.

Under the stretched modified Fermi coordinate, we get the following expression from the definition of gradient operator in Riemannian manifold(c.f.
[
9
]
):

\nabla v=\left(g^{11}v_{z}+g^{12}v_{s}\right)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}+\left(g^{21}v_{z}+g^{22}v_{s}\right)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}.

Then

A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\nabla v=\left(g^{11}v_{z}+g^{12}v_{s}\right)A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}+\left(g^{21}v_{z}+g^{22}v_{s}\right)A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}.

(2.7)

According to the method in linear algebra, we get

\displaystyle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[g^{11}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}

(2.8)

\displaystyle+\left[g^{21}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{22}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}

and

\displaystyle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[g^{11}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}

(2.9)

\displaystyle+\left[g^{21}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{22}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}.

Hence (
2.7
) can be written into the following form

A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\nabla v=\left(X_{1}v_{z}+X_{2}v_{s}\right)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}+\left(X_{2}v_{z}+X_{3}v_{s}\right)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},

where

\displaystyle X_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(g^{11}\right)^{2}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+2g^{11}g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left(g^{12}\right)^{2}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle X_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{11}g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+\left[\left(g^{12}\right)^{2}+g^{11}g^{22}\right]\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+g^{12}g^{22}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle

and

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(g^{12}\right)^{2}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+2g^{12}g^{22}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left(g^{22}\right)^{2}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle.

Recall
{A^{*}}
is the adjoint matrix of
A
. From (
2.8
), (
2.9
) and the method in linear algebra, we get

\displaystyle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{22}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}

\displaystyle-\left[g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{22}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}

and

\displaystyle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[g^{11}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}

\displaystyle+\left[g^{11}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle+g^{12}\left\langle A(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle\right]\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}.

Hence the following identities hold:

X_{1}=\frac{1}{\det g}\left\langle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle,

X_{2}=-\frac{1}{\det g}\left\langle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle

and

X_{3}=\frac{1}{\det g}\left\langle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle.

From direct computation, we get

\displaystyle\left\langle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+\varepsilon s\left[\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]+O\left(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}\right),

\left\langle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial z}\right\rangle=\varepsilon s\left[\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle+\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]+O\left(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}\right)

and

\left\langle{A^{*}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s},\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon}}{\partial s}\right\rangle=\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle+\varepsilon s\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,n\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}n,n\rangle+O\left(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}\right).

Then

X_{1}=\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+O\left(\varepsilon s\right),

X_{2}=-\varepsilon s\left(\frac{\langle{A^{*}}n,n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+\frac{\langle{A^{*}_{t}}n,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}\right)+O\left(\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\right)

and

\displaystyle X_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+\varepsilon s\left[\frac{\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}+\frac{2\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}-\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[\frac{1}{1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}}\left(\langle{A^{*}}n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle{A^{*}_{tt}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{2\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}}\left(\langle{A^{*}_{t}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle+2\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\frac{4\langle\gamma^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle^{2}}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{3}}-\frac{\langle n^{\prime},n^{\prime}\rangle}{\left(1-\langle\gamma^{\prime},n\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)\langle{A^{*}}\gamma^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime}\rangle\right]+O\left(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}\right).

According to the definition of divergence operator, we get

\displaystyle\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle X_{1}v_{zz}+2X_{2}v_{sz}+X_{3}v_{ss}+\left(\frac{\partial_{z}\det g}{2\det g}X_{1}+\frac{\partial_{s}\det g}{2\det g}X_{2}+\partial_{z}X_{1}+\partial_{s}X_{2}\right)v_{z}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\partial_{z}\det g}{2\det g}X_{2}+\frac{\partial_{s}\det g}{2\det g}X_{3}+\partial_{z}X_{2}+\partial_{s}X_{3}\right)v_{s}.

From direct computation, we get this lemma.
∎

Using the notation in Lemma
2.2
and (
3.3
), we rewrite Lemma
2.1
into the following form

Remark 2.3
.

Provided
\Gamma\subset\Omega
is a non-degenerate critical point of the functional
\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)
, there hold:

\mathbf{q}_{t}=\frac{1}{p+3}a_{32}\alpha^{2-p}\beta^{2}-\frac{2}{p+3}b_{21}\alpha^{2-p}\beta^{2},\quad\mathrm{on}\quad\Gamma

(2.10)

and the following problem has only trivial solution:

\displaystyle-a_{11}h^{\prime\prime}+\left[a_{22}-b_{11}-a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)\right]h^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\left[a_{22}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)-a_{33}+b_{22}+\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}\right]h=0.

(2.11)

The function
\Upsilon_{2}
,
\Upsilon_{1}
and
\Upsilon_{0}
are defined in (
8.3
) and (
8.4
). Let
h(\theta)=\beta(\theta)u(\theta)
in (
2.3
). we get

Remark 2.4
.

Under the condition that
\Gamma\subset\Omega
is a non-degenerate critical point of the functional
\mathcal{K}(\Gamma)
, we get the following problem also has only trivial solution.

-\Upsilon_{2}u^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}u^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}u=0,\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad(0,1).

3.
approximate solutions

In this section, we will construct a local approximate solution to (
1.9
). We look for a solution to (
1.9
) of the form

u(y)=\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(y)+v(y/\varepsilon).

Here
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(y)
is the unique negative solution to (
1.9
), whose property is studied in Proposition
B.1
. Then
v
solves the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla v)=|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon y)+v|^{p}-|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(\varepsilon y)|^{p},&\mbox{in $\Omega_{\varepsilon}$},\\
v=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}$},\end{array}\right.

(3.1)

where
\Omega_{\varepsilon}=\Omega/\varepsilon
.
Let

\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\theta,t)=-\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(\gamma(\theta)+tn(\theta))\qquad\mathrm{where}\qquad|t|<\delta_{0},\quad\theta\in[0,1].

Using Lemma
2.2
, we write the first equation in (
3.1
) into the following form in some neighborhood of the curve
\Gamma_{\varepsilon}

a_{11}(\varepsilon z)v_{zz}+a_{31}(\varepsilon z)v_{ss}+|v-\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)|^{p}-|\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)|^{p}+B_{1}(v)=0,

(3.2)

where
z\in[0,1/\varepsilon]
,
s\in(-\delta_{0}/\varepsilon,\delta_{0}/\varepsilon)
and

B_{1}(v)=2\varepsilon sa_{22}(\varepsilon z)v_{sz}+\left(\varepsilon sa_{32}(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}a_{33}(\varepsilon z)\right)v_{ss}+\varepsilon b_{11}(\varepsilon z)v_{z}+\left(\varepsilon b_{21}(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon^{2}sb_{22}(\varepsilon z)\right)v_{s}+B_{0}(v).

Let

\tilde{\alpha}(\theta)=\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\theta,0),\qquad\tilde{\beta}(\theta)=\frac{\left[\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\theta,0)\right]^{\frac{p-1}{2}}}{\left[a_{31}(\theta)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}}

and

\alpha(\theta)=\mathbf{q}(\theta,0),\qquad\beta(\theta)=\frac{\left[\mathbf{q}(\theta,0)\right]^{\frac{p-1}{2}}}{\left[a_{31}(\theta)\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(3.3)

From the argument in
[
34
]
, we get

\tilde{\alpha}(\theta)=\alpha(\theta)+O(\varepsilon^{2}),\qquad\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}(\theta)=\alpha^{\prime}(\theta)+O(\varepsilon^{2}),\qquad\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=\alpha^{\prime\prime}(\theta)+O(\varepsilon^{2})

(3.4)

and

\tilde{\beta}(\theta)=\beta(\theta)+O(\varepsilon^{2}),\qquad\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(\theta)=\beta^{\prime}(\theta)+O(\varepsilon^{2}),\qquad\tilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime}(\theta)=\beta^{\prime\prime}(\theta)+O(\varepsilon^{2}).

(3.5)

Let

v(z,s)=\tilde{\alpha}(\varepsilon z)u(z,x),\qquad\mathrm{where}\qquad x=\tilde{\beta}(\varepsilon z)s.

Then we get

v_{s}=\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\beta}u_{x},\qquad v_{ss}=\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\beta}^{2}u_{xx},

v_{z}=\varepsilon\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u+\tilde{\alpha}\left(u_{z}+\varepsilon\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}su_{x}\right),

v_{sz}=\varepsilon\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\tilde{\beta}u_{x}+\varepsilon\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}u_{x}+\tilde{\alpha}\tilde{\beta}\left(u_{xz}+\varepsilon\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}su_{xx}\right)

and

\displaystyle v_{zz}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}u+2\varepsilon\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\left(u_{z}+\varepsilon\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}su_{x}\right)+\tilde{\alpha}\left[u_{zz}+2\varepsilon\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}su_{xz}+\varepsilon^{2}(\tilde{\beta}^{\prime})^{2}s^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime}su_{x}\right].

It is easy to get

\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)=\tilde{\alpha}(\varepsilon z)+\varepsilon s\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t}(\varepsilon z,0)+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{tt}(\varepsilon z,0)+O(\varepsilon^{3}|s|^{3}).

Then we have

\displaystyle|v-\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)|^{p}-\left|\bar{\mathbf{q}}(\varepsilon z,\varepsilon s)\right|^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha}^{p}\left\{|u-1|^{p}-1-p\varepsilon s\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t}\left(|u-1|^{p-2}(u-1)+1\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}s^{2}\left[p(p-1)\tilde{\alpha}^{-2}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t}^{2}\left(|u-1|^{p-2}-1\right)-p\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{tt}\left(|u-1|^{p-2}(u-1)+1\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+O(\varepsilon^{3}|s|^{3}|u|^{\min\{p-3,1\}}).

The equation (
3.2
) is transformed into the following one:

S(u):=u_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{zz}+|u-1|^{p}-1+B_{4}(u)=0,

(3.6)

where
B_{4}(u)=B_{4}^{1}(u)+B_{4}^{2}(u)+B_{4}^{3}(u)
,
B_{4}^{1}(u)
and
B_{4}^{3}(u)
are linear functions of
u
. However
B_{4}^{2}(u)
is a nonlinear function of
u
. More precisely,

\displaystyle B_{4}^{1}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon a_{32}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}xu_{xx}+\varepsilon b_{21}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}u_{x}+2\varepsilon a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u_{z}+2\varepsilon a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{xz}+2\varepsilon a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}xu_{xz}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{z}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}u+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{x}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}(\tilde{\beta}^{\prime})^{2}x^{2}u_{xx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime}xu_{x}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}xu_{x}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{x}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}x^{2}u_{xx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}a_{33}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}x^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{2}b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u+\varepsilon^{2}b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{x}+\varepsilon^{2}b_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}xu_{x},

\displaystyle B_{4}^{2}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p\varepsilon\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t}x\left[|u-1|^{p-2}(u-1)+1\right]+\frac{p(p-1)}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\alpha}^{-2}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}(\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t})^{2}x^{2}\left[|u-1|^{p-2}-1\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{tt}x^{2}\left[|u-1|^{p-2}(u-1)+1\right]+O(\varepsilon^{3}s^{3}|u|^{\min\{p-3,1\}})

and

\displaystyle B_{4}^{3}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{zz}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u_{z}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{zx}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}u_{zx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{z}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}u+2\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}(\tilde{\beta}^{\prime})^{2}x^{2}u_{xx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime}xu_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{xx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}a_{33}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{3}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u+\varepsilon^{3}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}xu_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}b_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}u_{x}.

In order to construct a local approximate solution to (
3.6
), we introduce the parameters
\{f_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
and
\{e_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N}
. Assume following constrains holds:

\|f_{j}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\leq C|\log\varepsilon|^{2},\qquad\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})>\frac{2}{\sqrt{p}}|\ln\varepsilon|-\frac{4}{\sqrt{p}}\ln|\ln\varepsilon|

(3.7)

and

\|e_{j}\|_{*}:=\|e_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}+\varepsilon\|e_{j}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon^{2}\|e_{j}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}<\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(3.8)

We also denote
f_{0}=-\infty
and
f_{N+1}=+\infty
. Set

\mathbf{f}=(f_{1},\cdots,f_{N}),\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathbf{e}=(e_{1},\cdots,e_{N}).

Recall
w
is the unique solution of (
1.11
). Let
Z(x)
be the first eigenfunction of the following problem

Z^{\prime\prime}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)Z=\lambda_{0}Z,\quad Z(\pm\infty)=0,\quad\lambda_{0}>0

We define an approximate solution of (
3.6
) by

\mathcal{V}(x,z)=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}(z,x),

where

\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}(z,x):=\mathcal{V}_{k}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}(\varepsilon z)f_{k}(\varepsilon z))

and

\mathcal{V}_{k}(z,x)=w(x)+\varepsilon\varphi_{k}^{(1)}(\varepsilon z,x)+\varepsilon e_{k}(\varepsilon z)Z(x)+\varepsilon^{2}\varphi_{k}^{(2)}(\varepsilon z,x).

(3.9)

In (
3.9
), the function
\varphi_{k}^{(1)}
and
\varphi^{(2)}_{k}
will be determined in (
3.22
) and (
3.25
), respectively.
We assume that
\mathcal{V}_{k}
decays at infinity as
e^{-\sigma_{1}|x|}
for any constant
\sigma_{1}\in(0,\sqrt{p})
,.

From direct computation,

\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k,x}(z,x)=\mathcal{V}_{k,x}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k}),\qquad\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k,xx}(z,x)=\mathcal{V}_{k,xx}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k}),

\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k,z}(z,x)=\mathcal{V}_{k,z}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k})-\varepsilon(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}\mathcal{V}_{k,x}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k}),

\displaystyle\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k,zz}(z,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{k,zz}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k})-2\varepsilon(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}\mathcal{V}_{k,zx}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k})

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{V}_{k,x}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k})+\varepsilon^{2}|(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}|^{2}\mathcal{V}_{k,xx}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k})

and

\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k,zx}(z,x)=\mathcal{V}_{k,zx}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k})-\varepsilon(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}\mathcal{V}_{k,xx}(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k}).

Denote

S(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k})=\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})(z,x-\tilde{\beta}f_{k}).

(3.10)

Then we obtain

\tilde{S}(u)=u_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{zz}+|u-1|^{p}-1+B_{3}(u),

where
B_{3}(u)=B_{3}^{1}(u)+B_{3}^{2}(u)+B_{3}^{3}(u)
. More precisely

\displaystyle B_{3}^{1}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{zx}-\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime\prime}u_{x}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}|(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}|^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon b_{21}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}u_{x}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon a_{32}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{xx}+2\varepsilon a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u_{z}-2\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{x}

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{zx}-2\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{xx}

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{zx}-2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{xx}+\varepsilon b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{z}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{x}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}u+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}(\tilde{\beta}^{\prime})^{2}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}a_{33}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{2}u_{xx}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{2}b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}b_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}+\varepsilon^{2}b_{22}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x},

\displaystyle B_{3}^{2}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p\varepsilon\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})\left[|u-1|^{p-2}(u-1)+1\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{p(p-1)}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\alpha}^{-2}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{t}^{2}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{2}\left[|u-1|^{p-2}-1\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{2}\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{tt}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{2}\left[|u-1|^{p-2}(u-1)+1\right]+O(\varepsilon^{3}|x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k}|^{3}|u|^{\min\{p-3,1\}})

and

\displaystyle B_{3}^{3}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{zz}-2\varepsilon^{2}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{zx}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u_{z}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}u_{zx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}u_{z}+2\varepsilon^{2}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{zx}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}|(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}|^{2}u_{xx}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime\prime}u_{x}-2\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{x}-2\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{xx}

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{xx}-\varepsilon^{3}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{\prime}u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime\prime}u

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-2}(\tilde{\beta}^{\prime})^{2}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})^{2}u_{xx}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}a_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\beta}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}u_{x}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}a_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{3}a_{33}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{2}u_{xx}+\varepsilon^{3}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}u

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{3}b_{12}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}^{-1}\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}(x+\tilde{\beta}f_{k})u_{x}+\varepsilon^{3}b_{23}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\beta}u_{x}.

In the expression of
B_{3}^{3}(u)
, the functions
a_{ij}
and
b_{ij}
take values at
(\varepsilon z,\tilde{\beta}^{-1}x+f_{k})
.

Define

\mathcal{U}_{k}=\left\{(z,x)\in\mathfrak{S}\;:\;\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\beta}(\varepsilon z)\left[f_{k-1}(\varepsilon z)+f_{k}(\varepsilon z)\right]\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2}\tilde{\beta}(\varepsilon z)\left[f_{k}(\varepsilon z)+f_{k+1}(\varepsilon z)\right]\right\}

Now we use the method in
[
47
,
20
]
to estimate the nonlinear terms in
S(\mathcal{V})
. Using (
3.5
) and (
3.7
), we get for
(z,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{j}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p}-1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p}-1+p|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1)\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}+O\left(\left(\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p}-1\right)+\left[p|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1)\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-\sum_{k\not=j}(|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p}-1)\right]+O\left(\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p}-1\right)+p\left[|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1)+1\right]\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}+\max_{k\not=j}O\left(e^{-(2\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{k}|}\right),

where
\tilde{\sigma}>0
is a constant small enough.
In the same domain, we get the following estimate from the same method:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)+1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1)+1\right]+(p-1)\left(|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}-1\right)\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}+\max_{k\not=j}O\left(e^{-(2\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{k}|}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1)+1\right]+\max_{k\not=j}O\left(e^{-(2\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{k}|}+e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})(|x-\beta f_{k}|+|x-\beta f_{j}|)}\right)

and

|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}-1=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p-2}-1\right]+\max_{k\not=j}O\left(e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{k}|}\right).

Hence we arrive at

S(\mathcal{V})=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left[S(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j})+p\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}\left(|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1)+1\right)\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{\theta}_{j},

(3.11)

where
\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}
is a characteristic function which equals to
1
if
x\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
and equals to
0
if
x\not\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
, and

\displaystyle\tilde{\theta}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}\left[\max_{k\not=j}O\left(e^{-(2\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{k}|}\right)+\max_{k\not=j}O\left(\varepsilon|x|e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})(|x-\beta f_{k}|+|x-\beta f_{j}|)}\right)\right.

(3.12)

\displaystyle\left.+\max_{k\not=j}O\left(\varepsilon^{2}|x|^{2}e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{k}|}\right)\right].

Now we expand the term
\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})
. It is obvious that

|\mathcal{V}_{k}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}_{k}-1)+1=|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1+(p-1)\varepsilon|w-1|^{p-2}(\varphi_{k}^{(1)}+e_{k}Z)+O(\varepsilon^{2}e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x|}),

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{V}_{k}-1|^{p}-1=

\displaystyle|w-1|^{p}-1+p\varepsilon|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)(\varphi_{k}^{(1)}+e_{k}Z)+p\varepsilon^{2}|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(2)}_{k}

\displaystyle+\frac{p(p-1)}{2}\varepsilon^{2}|w-1|^{p-2}(\varphi_{k}^{(1)}+e_{k}Z)^{2}+O(\varepsilon^{3}e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x|}).

(3.13)

From (
3.4
), (
3.5
), (
3.9
) and direct computation, we get

\displaystyle B_{3}(\mathcal{V}_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left\{a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta xw_{xx}+b_{21}\alpha^{1-p}\beta w_{x}-p\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}f_{k}w_{xx}-p\alpha^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]\right\}+\varepsilon^{2}\left\{-a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime\prime}w_{x}\right.

\displaystyle-2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}w_{x}-2a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}xw_{xx}+b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+b_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}w_{x}

\displaystyle-2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}xw_{xx}-b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}w_{x}+2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+2a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}f_{k}xw_{xx}

\displaystyle+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+2a_{22}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta f_{k}w_{x}+p(p-1)\alpha^{-2}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}^{2}f_{k}x[|w-1|^{p-2}-1]

\displaystyle-p\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{tt}f_{k}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]+2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+2a_{33}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}xw_{xx}

\displaystyle+4a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}xw_{xx}-p(p-1)\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}(x+\beta f_{k})|w-1|^{p-2}\varphi_{k}^{(1)}+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta(x+\beta f_{k})\varphi^{(1)}_{k,xx}

\displaystyle+b_{21}\alpha^{1-p}\beta\varphi^{(1)}_{k,x}-2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}e_{k}^{\prime}Z^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}e_{k}^{\prime}f_{k}Z^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta e_{k}^{\prime}f_{k}Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle\left.+\hat{b}_{2}(e_{k})+\check{b}_{2}(e_{k},f_{k})\right\}+O(\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|^{8}e^{-\gamma_{1}|x|}).

In this expression,
\gamma_{1}>0
is a constant and
\hat{b}_{2}
is the combinations of
e_{k}
and some known functions, which is odd on
x
. However
\check{b}_{2}(e_{k},f_{k})
is even on
x
and it is a combinations of
f_{k}
,
e_{k}
and some known functions. In this paper, we consider
e_{k}
,
\varepsilon e_{k}^{\prime}
and
\varepsilon^{2}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}
as the same order.

From (
3.11
) and (
3
), we get

\displaystyle\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\lambda_{0}e_{k}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}\right)Z+\varepsilon\left[\varphi^{(1)}_{k,xx}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(1)}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{p(p-1)}{2}\varepsilon^{2}|w-1|^{p-2}(\varphi^{(1)}_{k}+e_{k}Z)^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\varphi^{(2)}_{k,xx}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(2)}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle+B_{3}(\mathcal{V}_{k})+O(\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|^{8}e^{-\gamma_{1}|x|})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{k}\right)Z(x)+\varepsilon\left[\varphi^{(1)}_{k,xx}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(1)}_{k}\right]+\varepsilon\left\{a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta xw_{xx}\right.

\displaystyle+b_{21}\alpha^{1-p}\beta w_{x}-p\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}f_{k}w_{xx}

\displaystyle\left.-p\alpha^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]\right\}+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\varphi^{(2)}_{k,xx}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(2)}_{k}\right]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\mathbf{A}_{k}+O(\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|^{8}e^{-\gamma_{1}|x|}),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime\prime}w_{x}-2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}w_{x}-2a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}xw_{xx}+b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}

\displaystyle+b_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}w_{x}-2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}xw_{xx}-b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}w_{x}+2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}

\displaystyle+2a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}f_{k}xw_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+2a_{22}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta f_{k}w_{x}

\displaystyle+p(p-1)\alpha^{-2}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}^{2}f_{k}x[|w-1|^{p-2}-1]-p\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{tt}f_{k}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]

\displaystyle+2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+2a_{33}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}xw_{xx}+4a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}xw_{xx}+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta(x+\beta f_{k})\varphi^{(1)}_{k,xx}

\displaystyle-p(p-1)\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}(x+\beta f_{k})|w-1|^{p-2}\varphi^{(1)}_{k}+b_{21}\alpha^{1-p}\beta\varphi^{(1)}_{k,x}-2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}e_{k}^{\prime}Z^{\prime}

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}e_{k}^{\prime}f_{k}Z^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta e_{k}^{\prime}f_{k}Z^{\prime}+\frac{p(p-1)}{2}|w-1|^{p-2}(\varphi^{(1)}_{k}+e_{k}Z)^{2}

\displaystyle+\hat{b}_{2}(e_{k})+\check{b}_{2}(e_{k},f_{k}).

In order to get a better approximate solution, we need to cancel the terms of the order
\varepsilon
in
\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})
. Then we solve the following equation

\displaystyle-\varphi^{(1)}_{k,xx}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(1)}_{k}

(3.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta xw_{xx}+b_{21}\alpha^{1-p}\beta w_{x}-p\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]

\displaystyle+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}f_{k}w_{xx}-p\alpha^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]=:\tilde{R}.

From (
1.11
) and direct computation, we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}}w=\frac{p+3}{2p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}xw_{xx}w_{x}dx=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2},

(3.15)

and

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]xw_{x}dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}w=-\frac{p+3}{2p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx.

(3.16)

It is well known that (
3.14
) is uniquely solvable provided

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{R}w_{x}dx=0.

(3.17)

With the help of (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), we see (
3.17
) is equivalent to

\mathbf{q}_{t}=\frac{1}{p+3}a_{32}\alpha^{2-p}\beta^{2}-\frac{2}{p+3}b_{21}\alpha^{2-p}\beta^{2}.

From Remark
2.3
, we get (
3.14
) is uniquely solvable.

Let
w_{0}
be the unique solution of the following problem

-w_{0,xx}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)w_{0}=w_{x}+\frac{2p}{p+3}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right],\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{0}w_{x}dx=0

(3.18)

and
w_{1}
be the unique solution of the following problem

-w_{1,xx}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)w_{1}=xw_{xx}-\frac{p}{p+3}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right],\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{1}w_{x}dx=0.

(3.19)

Let

w_{2}=-\frac{1}{2}xw_{x},\qquad w_{3}=\frac{1-p}{2p}xw_{x}-\frac{1}{p}w.

They solve the following problems respectively:

-w_{2,xx}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)w_{2}=w_{xx},\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{2}w_{x}dx=0

(3.20)

and

-w_{3,xx}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)w_{3}=|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{3}w_{x}dx=0.

(3.21)

Hence the solution of (
3.14
) is represented by

\varphi^{(1)}_{k}(\varepsilon z,x)=b_{21}\alpha^{1-p}\beta w_{0}+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta w_{1}+a_{32}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}f_{k}w_{2}-p\alpha^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}w_{3}.

(3.22)

With (
3.22
), we succeed to cancel the terms of the order
\varepsilon
in
\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})
. There holds

\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})=\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{k}\right)Z+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\varphi^{(2)}_{k,xx}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(2)}_{k}\right]+\varepsilon^{2}\mathbf{A}_{k}+O(\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|^{8}e^{-\gamma_{1}|x|}).

(3.23)

The sums of odd terms and that of even part terms in
\mathbf{A}_{k}
are denoted by
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{k}
and
\check{\mathbf{A}}_{k}
, respectively. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime\prime}w_{x}-2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}w_{x}-2a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}xw_{xx}-2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}xw_{xx}

(3.24)

\displaystyle-b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}w_{x}+2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+2a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}f_{k}xw_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime\prime}f_{k}w_{x}

\displaystyle+2a_{22}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta f_{k}w_{x}+p(p-1)\beta^{-1}\alpha^{-2}(\mathbf{q}_{t})^{2}f_{k}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}-1\right]+2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}

\displaystyle-p\beta^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{tt}f_{k}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]+2a_{33}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}xw_{xx}+4a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}xw_{xx}

\displaystyle+b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}w_{x}+b_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}w_{x}+b_{21}a_{32}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}f_{k}\left[w_{0,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{2}\right]

\displaystyle+(a_{32})^{2}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}f_{k}\left[w_{1,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{2}\right]+(a_{32})^{2}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}f_{k}xw_{2,xx}

\displaystyle-pa_{32}\alpha^{-p}\beta\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}\left[xw_{3,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{3}\right]+b_{21}a_{32}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}f_{k}w_{2,x}

\displaystyle-pb_{21}\alpha^{-p}\beta\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}w_{3,x}-p^{2}(p-1)b_{21}\alpha^{-p}\beta\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{3}

\displaystyle-p(p-1)a_{32}\alpha^{-p}\beta\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{2}+p^{2}(p-1)\alpha^{-2}\beta^{-1}(\mathbf{q}_{t})^{2}f_{k}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}

\displaystyle-p(p-1)b_{21}\alpha^{-p}\beta\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}-p(p-1)a_{32}\alpha^{-p}\beta\mathbf{q}_{t}f_{k}|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{k})^{\prime}e_{k}^{\prime}Z_{x}+2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{k}e_{k}^{\prime}Z_{x}+2\varepsilon a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{k}e_{k}^{\prime}Z_{x}+\hat{b}_{2}(e_{k}).

To cancel the even terms of the order
\varepsilon^{2}
, we consider the following problem

\varphi^{(2)}_{k,xx}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\varphi^{(2)}_{k}=-\check{\mathbf{A}}_{k}.

(3.25)

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\mathbf{A}}_{k}w_{x}dx=0
, it is uniquely solvable.

Hence we get

\tilde{S}(\mathcal{V}_{k})=\varepsilon\left[\lambda_{0}e_{k}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}\right]Z+\varepsilon^{2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{k}+\varepsilon^{3}\mathbf{B}_{k},

(3.26)

where
|\mathbf{B}_{k}|\leq C|\log\varepsilon|^{8}e^{-\gamma_{1}|x|}
.

Let

w_{j}(z,x)=w(x-\beta f_{j}),\quad Z_{j}(z,x)=Z(x-\beta f_{j}),\quad\mathrm{for}\quad j=1,2,\cdots,N.

(3.27)

In the case of
(z,x)\in\mathcal{U}_{j}
, we get the following estimate from Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle\left[|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1)+1\right]\sum_{k\not=j}\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1|^{p-2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j}-1)+1\right]\left(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j-1}+\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{j+1}\right)+O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{j}|})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)+1\right](w_{j-1}+w_{j+1})+\max_{k\not=j}O(\varepsilon e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})(|x-\beta f_{j}|+|x-\beta f_{k}|)})+O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}e^{-\tilde{\sigma}|x-\beta f_{j}|})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{p}\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)+1\right]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j+1})}\right)+O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{j}|})

\displaystyle+\max_{k\not=j}O(\varepsilon e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})(|x-\beta f_{j}|+|x-\beta f_{k}|)}).

Hence

\displaystyle S(\mathcal{V})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}\varepsilon\left(\lambda_{0}e_{j}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{j}^{\prime\prime}\right)Z_{j}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\varepsilon^{2}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{j}(z,x-\beta f_{j})+\varepsilon^{3}\mathbf{B}_{j}(z,x-\beta f_{j})+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\tilde{\theta}_{j}

(3.28)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}\left[p\alpha_{p}\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)+1\right]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j+1})}\right)+\tilde{\theta}_{j2}\right],

where

\tilde{\theta}_{j2}=\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}\left[O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})|x-\beta f_{j}|})+\max_{k\not=j}O(\varepsilon e^{-(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\sigma})(|x-\beta f_{j}|+|x-\beta f_{k}|)})\right]

(3.29)

Denote

\mathcal{E}_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\varepsilon\left(\lambda_{0}e_{j}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{j}^{\prime\prime}\right)Z_{j}\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathcal{E}_{2}=S(\mathcal{V})-\mathcal{E}_{1}.

Let
\mathfrak{S}=\left\{(z,x):z\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon},x\in\mathbb{R}\right\}
. Then we get

\|\mathcal{E}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu},

(3.30)

where
\mu>0
is constant small enough.
It follows from direct computation(see
[
41
]
for the method). For example, a typical term is

K_{1}=p\alpha_{p}\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)+1\right]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j+1})}\right).

It is easy to get

\displaystyle\|K_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{1/\varepsilon}dz\int_{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j}+f_{j-1})}^{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j}+f_{j+1})}\left|[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)+1]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j+1})}\right)\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{1/\varepsilon}dz\int_{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j-1}-f_{j})}^{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\left|[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}e^{-\sqrt{p}t}+e^{\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}e^{\sqrt{p}t}\right)\right|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{1/\varepsilon}dz\int_{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j-1}-f_{j})}^{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\left|[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}e^{-\sqrt{p}t}\right|^{2}dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1/\varepsilon}dz\int_{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j-1}-f_{j})}^{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\left|[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}e^{-\sqrt{p}t}\right|^{2}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{3}|\log\varepsilon|^{2q}.

Hence
\|K_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3/2}|\log\varepsilon|^{q}
.

In fact,
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is of the following form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left[S(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k})-\varepsilon\left(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{k}\right)Z_{k}\right]+\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p}-1-\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(|\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}-1|^{p}-1\right)\right]

\displaystyle+B_{4}^{2}(\mathcal{V})-\sum_{k=1}^{N}B_{4}^{2}(\bar{\mathcal{V}}_{k}).

By estimating the derivatives of
\mathcal{E}_{2}
with respect to
f_{k}
and
e_{k}
, we get

\|\mathcal{E}_{2}(\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1})-\mathcal{E}_{2}(\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

(3.31)

4.
The gluing procedure

Fix a positive constant
\delta<\delta_{0}/100
. Let
\eta_{\delta}
be a smooth truncated function satisfying
\eta_{\delta}(t)=1
for
t<\delta
and
\eta_{\delta}(t)=0
for
t>2\delta
. Let
\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}(x)=\eta_{\delta}(\varepsilon|x|)
. We define a global approximate solution of (
3.1
) by

\mathtt{w}(y)=\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}(x)\tilde{\alpha}(\varepsilon z)\mathcal{V}(z,x).

(4.1)

Then
v=\mathtt{w}+\tilde{\phi}
solves (
3.1
) if and only if
\tilde{\phi}
solves the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\tilde{L}(\tilde{\phi})=\tilde{E}+\tilde{N}(\tilde{\phi}),&\mbox{in $\Omega_{\varepsilon}$,}\\
\tilde{\phi}=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}$,}\end{array}\right.

(4.2)

where

\tilde{L}(\tilde{\phi})=\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla\tilde{\phi})+p|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\tilde{\phi},

\tilde{E}=\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla\mathtt{w})+|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}-|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}

and
