\tilde{N}(\tilde{\phi})=|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}+\tilde{\phi}|^{p}-|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}-p|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\tilde{\phi}.

We look for the solution to (
4.2
) of the following form

\tilde{\phi}=\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\varphi+\psi,

where
\varphi
is defined in some neighborhood of
\Gamma_{\varepsilon}
.

From direct computation, we know
\tilde{\phi}
is a solution of (
4.2
) if the pair
(\varphi,\psi)
solves the following problems:

-\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{L}(\varphi)=\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{E}+\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{N}(\varphi+\psi)+p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\psi-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|\psi

(4.3)

and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla\psi)-p(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon})|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\psi-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\psi\\
\qquad\qquad=(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon})\tilde{E}+(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon})\tilde{N}(\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\varphi+\psi)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Div(A(\varepsilon y)\nabla\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon})\varphi+2\langle A(\varepsilon y)\nabla\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon},\nabla\varphi\rangle,&\mbox{in $\Omega_{\varepsilon}$}\\
\psi=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}$}.\end{array}\right.

(4.4)

We call (
4.4
) the outer problem and (
4.3
) the inner problem.

4.1.
Outer problem

In order to solve the outer problem (
4.4
), we consider the following problem first

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Div(A(\varepsilon x)\nabla\psi)-(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon})p|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\psi-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}\psi=h,&\mbox{in $\Omega_{\varepsilon}$}\\
\psi=0&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon}$}\end{array}\right.

(4.5)

Lemma 4.1
.

Provided
h\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})
and
\varepsilon>0
small enough, (
4.5
) has a unique solution
\psi
, which satisfies the following estimate

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\leq C\sup_{y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}}\|h\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(y)\cap\Omega_{\varepsilon})},

(4.6)

where the constant
C>0
is independent of
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

According to the standard theory of elliptic partial differential equations( c.f.
[
23
]
), we know (
4.5
) has a unique solution. We only need to prove the priori estimate (
4.6
).

Suppose this estimate does not hold. There exist
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
, and the function
h_{n}
with the property
\sup_{y\in\Omega_{\varepsilon}}\|h_{n}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(y)\cap\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\to 0
, such that the solution
\psi_{n}
of (
4.5
) corresponding to
h=h_{n}
satisfies
\|\psi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\varepsilon_{n}})}=1
.

There exist
x_{n}\in\Omega
, so that
\psi_{n}(x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n})>1/2
. Since
\psi_{n}
satisfies Dirichlet condition, we get
x_{n}\to x_{0}\in\Omega
.

Let

\bar{\psi}_{n}(y)=\psi_{n}(y+x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n}).

Then it is the solution of the following problem

\displaystyle-\Div(A(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)\nabla\bar{\psi}_{n})-p\eta_{\delta}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)|^{p-2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y))\bar{\psi}_{n}

\displaystyle\qquad-p(1-\eta_{\delta}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y))|\mathtt{w}(y+x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n})+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)|^{p-2}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad(\mathtt{w}(y+x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n})+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y))\bar{\psi}_{n}=\bar{h}_{n},\quad\mathrm{in}\quad\tilde{\Omega}_{\varepsilon_{n}},

(4.7)

\bar{\psi}_{n}=0\qquad\mathrm{on}\qquad\partial\tilde{\Omega}_{\varepsilon_{n}},

where

\tilde{\Omega}_{\varepsilon_{n}}=(\Omega-x_{n})/\varepsilon_{n},\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\bar{h}_{n}(y)=h_{n}(y+x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n}).

For any smooth bounded domain
D
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we get
\|\bar{h}_{n}\|_{L^{2}(D)}
is uniformly bounded for
n
large enough. From elliptic estimate,
\|\bar{\phi}_{n}\|_{H^{2}(D^{\prime})}
is uniformly bounded, for any compact set
D^{\prime}\subset\subset D
. Hence
\|\bar{\phi}_{n}\|_{C^{0,\gamma}(D^{\prime})}
is uniformly bounded. With the help of Proposition
B.1
, we get
\bar{\psi}_{n}
converges to the solution of the following problem on compact sets:

-\Div(A(x_{0})\nabla\psi_{0})+p\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x_{0})\psi_{0}=0,\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(4.8)

In fact, by multiplying the both sides of (
4.1
) by
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Div(A(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)\nabla\phi)\bar{\psi}_{n}dy-p\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)|^{p-2}(\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y))\bar{\psi}_{n}\phi dy

\displaystyle\qquad-p\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\eta_{\delta}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y))|\mathtt{w}(y+x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n})+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y)|^{p-2}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad(\mathtt{w}(y+x_{n}/\varepsilon_{n})+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon_{n}}(x_{n}+\varepsilon_{n}y))\bar{\psi}_{n}\phi dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{h}_{n}\phi dy.

Let
n\to+\infty
. Then we get

-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Div(A(x_{0})\nabla\phi)\psi_{0}-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Psi^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x_{0})\psi_{0}\phi=0.

Hence
\psi_{0}
is the solution to (
4.8
). Then
\psi_{0}=0
, which is a contradiction.
∎

Assume
\varphi
satisfies the following decay condition

\sup_{|x|\geq\frac{3\delta}{\varepsilon}-1}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(z,x))}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|\geq 3\delta/\varepsilon)}\leq e^{-\sqrt{p}\delta/(4\varepsilon)}.

(4.9)

From Lemma
4.1
and fixed point theorem, we get (
4.4
) has a unique solution
\psi=\psi(\varphi)
, which satisfies the following estimate

\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}\leq C\left[e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{2\varepsilon}}+\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\min\{p,2\}}+\varepsilon\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>3\delta/\varepsilon)}+\varepsilon\sup_{|x|\geq\frac{3\delta}{\varepsilon}-1}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(z,x))}\right],

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle\|\psi(\varphi_{1})-\psi(\varphi_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon\left[\|\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>3\delta/\varepsilon)}+\sup_{|x|\geq\frac{3\delta}{\varepsilon}-1}\|\nabla\varphi_{1}-\nabla\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(z,x))}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C\left[\|\varphi_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\min\{p-1,1\}}+\|\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\right]\|\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

4.2.
Inner problem

After solving the outer problem (
4.4
) for given
\varphi
satisfying (
4.9
), we only need to solve the following problem

-\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{L}(\varphi)=\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{E}+\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{N}(\varphi+\psi(\varphi))+p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\psi(\varphi)-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|\psi(\varphi).

(4.11)

Let

\varphi(z,s)=\tilde{\alpha}(\varepsilon z)\hat{\varphi}(z,x),\quad\mathrm{where}\quad x=\tilde{\beta}(\varepsilon z)s.

In
(z,x)
coordinate, we have

\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\tilde{L}(\varphi)=\hat{\varphi}_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\hat{\varphi}_{zz}+B_{4}^{1}(\hat{\varphi})+B_{4}^{3}(\hat{\varphi})+p\left|\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)\hat{\varphi}.

Now we extend the operator on the right hand side of the equality above to the whole
\mathfrak{S}
. Let

\mathcal{L}(\phi)=\phi_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\phi_{zz}+p|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\phi+B_{5}(\phi),

(4.12)

where

B_{5}(\phi)=\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left(B_{4}^{1}(\phi)+B_{4}^{3}(\phi)\right)+p\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[\left|\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)-|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\right]\phi.

Rather than solving problem (
4.11
), we consider the following problem

-\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})=\tilde{\alpha}^{-p}\left[\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{E}+\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{N}(\varphi+\psi(\varphi))+p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}(\mathtt{w}+\bar{u}_{\varepsilon})\psi(\varphi)-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|^{p-2}|\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}|\psi(\varphi)\right],

which is equivalent to the following one:

\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})=-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\hat{\varphi}+\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi}))-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[\left|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)+\left|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)\right]\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi}),

(4.13)

where
\psi(z,s)=\tilde{\alpha}(\varepsilon z)\hat{\psi}(z,x)
and

\hat{N}(\phi)=|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}+\phi|^{p}-|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p}-p|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})\phi.

We first solve the following projective version of (
4.13
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[\left|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)+\left|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)\right]\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi})

(4.14)

\displaystyle-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{E}_{2}-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\hat{\varphi}+\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi}))+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j},

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}(z,x)w_{j,x}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}(z,x)Z_{j}(x)dx=0,

(4.15)

\hat{\varphi}(0,x)=\hat{\varphi}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\hat{\varphi}_{z}(0,x)=\hat{\varphi}_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),

(4.16)

where the term
\mathcal{E}_{1}
is absorbed in
\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j}
. Here we recall
w_{j}
and
Z_{j}
are defined in (
3.27
).

5.
Linear theory

In order to solve problem (
4.14
)-(
4.16
), we consider the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}L(\phi)=h+\sum_{j=1}^{N}c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j},&\mbox{in $\mathfrak{S}$,}\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)w_{j,x}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)Z_{j}(x)dx=0,&z\in(0,1/\varepsilon),\\
\phi(0,x)=\phi(1/\varepsilon,x),\quad\phi_{z}(0,x)=\phi_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),&x\in\mathbb{R},\end{array}\right.

(5.1)

where

L(\phi)=\phi_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\phi_{zz}+p|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\phi.

Let

\phi(z,x)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{j}+\tilde{\psi},\qquad\eta_{j}(z,x)=\eta\left(\frac{x-\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{j}(\varepsilon z)}{R}\right)

where
R=\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}|\log\varepsilon|
,
\eta(r)
is a smooth cutoff function such that
\eta(r)=1
for
|r|<1/2
and
\eta(r)=0
for
|r|>5/6
. Then
\phi
solves (
5.1
) if and only if the functions
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
,
\tilde{\psi}
solve the following equations:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{j,xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\phi}_{j,zz}+p|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)\tilde{\phi}_{j}

(5.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{j}h+p\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)-|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{j}

\displaystyle-p\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\psi}+c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}+d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j},

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(z,x)w_{j,x}(x)dx=\Lambda_{j1},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(z,x)Z_{j}(x)dx=\Lambda_{j2},\qquad z\in\left(0,\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right),

(5.3)

\tilde{\phi}_{j}(0,x)=\tilde{\phi}_{j}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\tilde{\phi}_{j,z}(0,x)=\tilde{\phi}_{j,z}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}

(5.4)

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\psi}_{zz}+p\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\right)|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\tilde{\psi}-p\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\tilde{\psi}

(5.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\right)h-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\eta_{j,xx}\tilde{\phi}_{j}+2\eta_{j,x}\tilde{\phi}_{j,x}\right)-a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\eta_{j,zz}\tilde{\phi}_{j}+2\eta_{j,z}\tilde{\phi}_{j,z}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{N}(1-\eta_{j})c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}(1-\eta_{j})d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j},

where

\Lambda_{j1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\eta_{j})\tilde{\phi}_{j}w_{j,x}dx-\sum_{k\not=j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{k}\tilde{\phi}_{k}w_{j,x}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\psi}w_{j,x}dx,

\Lambda_{j2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\eta_{j})\tilde{\phi}_{j}Z_{j}dx-\sum_{k\not=j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{k}\tilde{\phi}_{k}Z_{j}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\psi}Z_{j}dx,

\tilde{\chi}_{j}(z,x)=\chi\left(\frac{x-\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{j}(\varepsilon z)}{R}\right)

and
\chi(r)
is a smooth cutoff function such that
\chi(r)=1
for
|r|<5/6
and
\chi(r)=0
for
|r|>7/8
.

In order to solve these problems, we consider the following problem first

\phi_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\phi_{zz}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\phi=h,\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathfrak{S},

(5.6)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)w_{x}(x)dx=\Lambda_{1}(z);\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)Z(x)dx=\Lambda_{3}(z),\qquad z\in(0,1/\varepsilon),

(5.7)

\phi(0,x)=\phi(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\phi_{z}(0,x)=\phi_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.8)

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists a constant
C>0
independent of
\varepsilon>0
such that the solution
\phi
to (
5.6
)-(
5.8
) satisfies the following priori estimate

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right].

(5.9)

Proof.

We first consider the case of
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}=0
. According to
[
29
, Theorem 1.5.1]
, we expand
\phi
and
h
into the following form

\phi(z,x)=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\phi_{k}(x)\upsilon_{k}(z),\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad h(z,x)=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}h_{k}(x)\upsilon_{k}(z),

(5.10)

where
\upsilon_{k}
is the unit
L^{2}
eigenfunction of the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\upsilon_{k}^{\prime\prime}=\mu_{k}\upsilon_{k},&\mbox{in $(0,1/\varepsilon)$},\\
\upsilon_{k}(0)=\upsilon_{k}(1/\varepsilon),\end{array}\right.

and
\mu_{k}\geq 0
.
Then
\phi_{k}
and
h_{k}
satisfy the following equations

\phi_{k,xx}-\mu_{k}\phi_{k}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\phi_{k}=h_{k}\quad\mathrm{in}\quad\mathbb{R},

(5.11)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{k}(x)w_{x}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{k}(x)Z(x)dx=0.

(5.12)

Multiplying the both sides of (
5.11
) by
\phi_{k}
and integrating, we have

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\left[|\phi_{k,x}|^{2}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\phi_{k}^{2}\right]dx+\mu_{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{k}^{2}dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{k}\phi_{k}dx.

Since (
5.12
) holds, we get

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\left[|\phi_{k,x}|^{2}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\phi_{k}^{2}\right]dx\geq C\left[\|\phi_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}+\|\phi_{k,x}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\right].

Then we arrive at

\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\phi_{k,x}|^{2}dx+(1+\mu_{k})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{k}^{2}dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{k}^{2}dx.

(5.13)

From the expression (
5.10
), we get

\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\phi_{k}|^{2}dx,\qquad\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\phi_{k,x}|^{2}dx

and

\int_{\mathfrak{S}}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\phi_{zz}\phi dxdz=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu_{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\phi_{k}|^{2}dx,\qquad\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h_{k}|^{2}dx.

With the help of (
5.13
) and these identities above, we get

\|\phi\|_{H^{1}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

From elliptic estimate,

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

Hence in the case of
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}=0
, (
5.9
) holds.

In the general case, we define

\bar{\phi}(z,x)=\phi(z,x)-\frac{\Lambda_{1}(z)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}w_{x}(x)-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(z)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}Z(x).

It is the solution of the following problem

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\bar{\phi}_{zz}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\bar{\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h-\frac{a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\Lambda_{1}^{\prime\prime}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}w_{x}-\frac{a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\Lambda_{2}^{\prime\prime}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}Z(x)

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda_{0}\Lambda_{2}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}Z(x),\qquad\qquad\mbox{in $\mathfrak{S}$},

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{\phi}(x)w_{x}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{\phi}(x)Z(x)dx=0,\qquad z\in(0,1/\varepsilon).

Using the conclusion in the case
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}=0
, we get

\|\bar{\phi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right].

Then (
5.9
) holds.
∎

Now we consider the following projective version

\phi_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\phi_{zz}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\phi=h+c(\varepsilon z)w_{x}(x)+d(\varepsilon z)Z(x),\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathfrak{S},

(5.14)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)w_{x}(x)dx=\Lambda_{1}(z),\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)Z(x)dx=\Lambda_{2}(z),\qquad z\in(0,1/\varepsilon),

(5.15)

\phi(0,x)=\phi(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\phi_{z}(0,x)=\phi_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.16)

Lemma 5.2
.

Provided
h\in L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})
and
\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}\in H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)
, (
5.14
)-(
5.16
) has a unique solution
\phi=T_{0}(h,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
. Moreover, it satisfies the following estimate

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right].

(5.17)

Proof.

We first consider the case of
\Lambda_{1}=\Lambda_{2}=0
. Write
h
into the following form

h(z,x)=\sum_{k=0}^{+\infty}h_{k}(x)\upsilon_{k}(z).

In order to solve (
5.14
)-(
5.16
), we consider the following problem

\phi_{k,xx}-\mu_{k}\phi_{k}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\phi_{k}=h_{k}(x)+c_{k}w_{x}(x)+d_{k}Z(x),\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathbb{R},

(5.18)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{k}w_{x}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{k}Zdx=0,

(5.19)

where
c_{k}
and
d_{k}
are constants. From Fredholm alternative, we get (
5.18
)-(
5.19
) has a unique solution
\phi_{k}
, with

c_{k}=-\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{k}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx},\qquad d_{k}=-\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{k}Zdx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}.

It is easy to get

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|c_{k}|^{2}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2},\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|d_{k}|^{2}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}.

Let

\phi(z,x)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\phi_{k}(x)\upsilon_{k}(z),

c(\varepsilon z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}\upsilon_{k}(z),\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad d(\varepsilon z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}d_{k}\upsilon_{k}(z).

Then
\phi
is the unique solution of problem (
5.14
)-(
5.16
).

However, in the general case, we define

\bar{\phi}(z,x)=\phi(z,x)-\frac{\Lambda_{1}(z)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}w_{x}(x)-\frac{\Lambda_{2}(z)}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}Z(x).

Then (
5.14
)-(
5.16
) is transformed into the following problem

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\bar{\phi}_{zz}+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\bar{\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h-\frac{a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\Lambda_{1}^{\prime\prime}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}w_{x}-\frac{a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\Lambda_{2}^{\prime\prime}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}Z(x)

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda_{0}\Lambda_{2}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z^{2}dx}Z(x)+c(\varepsilon z)w_{x}+d(\varepsilon z)Z(x),\quad\mathrm{in}\quad\mathfrak{S},

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{\phi}(x)w_{x}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bar{\phi}(x)Z(x)dx=0,\qquad z\in(0,1/\varepsilon).

It is uniquely solvable according to the argument above. The priori estimate (
5.17
) follows from Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Now we consider the following problem

\phi_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\phi_{zz}+p|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)\phi=h+c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}(x)+d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j}(x),\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathfrak{S},

(5.20)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)w_{j,x}(x)dx=\Lambda_{1}(z),\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)Z_{j}(x)dx=\Lambda_{2}(z),\qquad z\in(0,1/\varepsilon),

(5.21)

\phi(0,x)=\phi(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\phi_{z}(0,x)=\phi_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.22)

Lemma 5.3
.

Given
h\in L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})
, (
5.20
)-(
5.22
) has a unique solution
\phi=T_{j}(h,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
. The solution
\phi
satisfies the following estimate

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right].

(5.23)

Moreover, the operator
T_{j}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
,
i.e.

\|T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{1}}-T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{2}}\|\leq C\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}.

(5.24)

Proof.

Let

\tilde{\phi}(z,x)=\phi(z,x+\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{j}(\varepsilon z)),\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\tilde{h}(z,x)=h(z,x+\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{j}(\varepsilon z)).

Then (
5.20
)-(
5.22
) is transformed into the following problem

\tilde{\phi}_{xx}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\tilde{\phi}_{zz}+B_{6}(\tilde{\phi})+p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)\tilde{\phi}=\tilde{h}+c_{j}w_{x}+d_{j}Z,\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathfrak{S},

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\phi}(z,x)w_{x}(x)dx=\Lambda_{1}(z);\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\phi}(z,x)Z(x)dx=\Lambda_{2}(z),

\tilde{\phi}(0,x)=\tilde{\phi}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\tilde{\phi}_{z}(0,x)=\tilde{\phi}_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad x\in\mathbb{R},

where

B_{6}(\tilde{\phi})=a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\left[\varepsilon^{2}|(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}|^{2}\tilde{\phi}_{xx}-\varepsilon^{2}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{x}-2\varepsilon(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}\tilde{\phi}_{zx}\right]+a_{11}(\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}-\alpha^{1-p})\tilde{\phi}_{zz}.

We write this problem into the following fixed point problem

\tilde{\phi}=T_{0}(\tilde{h}-B_{6}(\tilde{\phi}),\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2}).

(5.25)

We get
\|B_{6}(\tilde{\phi})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}
via direct computation along with
[
8
, Theorem 8.8]
. From fixed point theorem, (
5.25
) has a unique solution. Moreover, it satisfies (
5.23
) from Lemma
5.2
.

Now we estimate the Lipschitz property of
T_{j}
. Let
\phi_{i}=T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{i},\mathbf{e}_{i}}(h,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{2})
,
i=1,2
. Then
\phi_{i}
is the solution of the following problem

\phi_{i,xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\phi_{i,zz}+p|w_{ji}-1|^{p-2}(w_{ji}-1)\phi_{i}=h+c_{ji}w_{ji,x}+d_{ji}Z_{ji},\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathfrak{S},

(5.26)

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{i}(z,x)w_{ji}dx=\Lambda_{1},\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{i}(z,x)Z_{ji}dx=\Lambda_{2},

\phi_{i}(0,x)=\phi_{i}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\phi_{i,z}(0,x)=\phi_{i,z}(1/\varepsilon,x),

where

w_{ji}(z,x)=w(x-\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{ji}(\varepsilon z)),\qquad Z_{ji}(z,x)=Z(x-\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{ji}(\varepsilon z))

Let
\phi^{*}=\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}
. Then
\phi^{*}
satisfies the following equations:

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\phi^{*}_{zz}+p|w_{j1}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j1}-1)\phi^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{j2}(w_{j1,x}-w_{j2,x})+d_{j2}(Z_{j1}-Z_{j2})+(c_{j1}-c_{j2})w_{j1,x}+(d_{j1}-d_{j2})Z_{j1}

\displaystyle\quad-p\left[|w_{j1}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j1}-1)-|w_{j2}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j2}-1)\right]\phi_{2},\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathfrak{S},

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi^{*}w_{j1,x}dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{2}(w_{j1,x}-w_{j2,x})dx,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi^{*}Z_{j1}dx=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{2}(Z_{j1}-Z_{j2})dx,

\phi^{*}(0,x)=\phi^{*}(1/\varepsilon,x),\qquad\phi^{*}_{z}(0,x)=\phi^{*}_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x).

Multiplying the both sides of (
5.26
) by
w_{ji,x}
and
Z_{ji}
, respectively and integrating, we get

a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\Lambda_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}hw_{ji,x}dx+c_{ji}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx

and

a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\Lambda_{2}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}\Lambda_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}hZ_{ji}dx+d_{ji}.

Then we get

\|c_{ji}\|_{L^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\leq C\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

and

\|d_{ji}\|_{L^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\leq C\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

From this fact, we have

\displaystyle\|c_{j2}(w_{j1,x}-w_{j2,x})+d_{j2}(Z_{j1}-Z_{j2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[\|c_{ji}\|_{L^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|d_{ji}\|_{L^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right]\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right].

Denote

\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}(z)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{2}(z,x)(w_{j1,x}-w_{j2,x})dx,\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}(z)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{2}(z,x)(Z_{j1}-Z_{j2})dx

From direct computation, we get

\|\tilde{\Lambda}_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\tilde{\Lambda}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\leq C\|\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}

and

\|p[|w_{j1}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j1}-1)-|w_{j2}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j2}-1)]\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\|\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

From the priori estimate in (
5.23
), we arrive at

\|\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}(S)}\leq C\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\right].

Hence (
5.24
) holds.
∎

Proposition 5.4
.

Problem (
5.1
) has a unique solution
\phi=\tilde{T}(h)
, where
\tilde{T}
is a linear operator and
\phi
satisfies the following estimate

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(5.27)

Moreover,
\tilde{T}
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
:

\|\tilde{T}_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}}-\tilde{T}_{\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2}}\|\leq C\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

(5.28)

Proof.

According to the argument above, we only need to solve the system (
5.2
)-(
5.4
) and the problem (
5.5
).
With Lemma
5.3
, we first consider problem (
5.2
)-(
5.4
) for fixed function
\tilde{\psi}
. It is written into the following problem

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{j}\left[\tilde{\chi}_{j}h+p\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)-|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{j}\right.

(5.29)

\displaystyle\left.-p\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\psi},\Lambda_{j1},\Lambda_{j2}\right],\qquad j=1,2,\cdots,N.

From tedious computation and Sobolev imbbeding theorem in
[
8
, Theorem 8.8]
, we get

\|\Lambda_{j1}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}+\|\Lambda_{j2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1/\varepsilon)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\tilde{\phi}_{k}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+C\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})},

\|p[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)+1]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

and

\|p\left[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)-|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

Using the fixed point theorem, we get (
5.29
) has a unique solution
\tilde{\phi}_{j}=\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})
,
j=1,2,\cdots,N
. Moreover
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
satisfies the following estimate

\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\right].

(5.30)

It is easy to get
\phi_{j}
is Lipschitz dependent on
\psi
:

\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi}_{1})-\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi}_{2})\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(5.31)

By replacing
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
by
\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})
in (
5.5
), we consider the following problem

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\tilde{\psi}_{zz}+p\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\right)|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\tilde{\psi}-p\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\tilde{\psi}

(5.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\right)h-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\eta_{j,xx}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})+2\eta_{j,x}\tilde{\phi}_{j,x}(\tilde{\psi})\right)-a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left(\eta_{j,zz}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})+2\eta_{j,z}\tilde{\phi}_{j,z}(\tilde{\psi})\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{N}(1-\eta_{j})c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}(1-\eta_{j})d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j}.

It is easy to see that for
\varepsilon>0
small enough,

p\left(1-\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\right)|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\tilde{\psi}-p\sum_{j=1}^{N}\eta_{j}\tilde{\psi}\leq-\frac{p}{2}

Now we consider the solutions to (
5.32
) via fixed point theorem.

Integrating the both sides of (
5.2
) by
w_{j,x}
, we get

\displaystyle c_{j}(\varepsilon z)\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\chi}_{j}hw_{j,x}dx+p\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)-|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\phi}_{j}w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle+p\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j}\tilde{\psi}w_{j,x}dx.

Then we get

\|(1-\eta_{j})c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\right].

From the same method, we also get

\|(1-\eta_{j})d_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\left[\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\right].

With the help of Sobolev imbedding theorem, we have

\|\eta_{j,xx}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})+2\eta_{j,x}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq\frac{C}{|\log\varepsilon|^{1/2}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

and

\left\|a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\left(\eta_{j,zz}\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})+2\eta_{j,x}\tilde{\phi}_{j,x}(\tilde{\psi})\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

Using the fixed point theorem along with (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), we see (
5.5
) has a unique solution. Moreover, it satisfies the estimate

\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(5.33)

Then (
5.1
) has a unique solution, which satisfies the estimate (
5.27
).

Now we prove the estimate (
5.28
). We will prove this fact by estimating the Lipschitz dependence of
\tilde{\phi}_{j}
and
\tilde{\psi}
on
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
.

Claim:

\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\tilde{\psi})
is Lipschitz continuous on
\mathbf{f}
,
\mathbf{e}
and
\tilde{\psi}
:

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1},\tilde{\psi}_{1})-\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2},\tilde{\psi}_{2})\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

(5.34)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right]+C\|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

Denote
\tilde{\phi}_{ji}=\tilde{\phi}_{j}(\mathbf{f}_{i},\mathbf{e}_{i},\tilde{\psi}_{i})
,
i=1,2
. Then it satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}_{ji}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{i}}\left\{\tilde{\chi}_{ji}h+p\left[|w_{ji}-1|^{p-2}(w_{ji}-1)-|\mathcal{V}^{(i)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{ji}\tilde{\phi}_{ji}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-p\left[|\mathcal{V}^{(i)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(i)}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\chi}_{ji}\tilde{\psi}_{i},\Lambda_{j1}^{(i)},\Lambda_{j2}^{(i)}\right\},

where

\tilde{\chi}_{ji}=\chi\left(\frac{x-\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{ji}(\varepsilon z)}{R}\right),\qquad\mathcal{V}^{(i)}=\mathcal{V}(\mathbf{f}_{i},\mathbf{e}_{i}),

\Lambda_{j1}^{(i)}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\eta_{ji})\tilde{\phi}_{j}w_{ji,x}dx-\sum_{k\not=j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{ki}\tilde{\phi}_{ki}w_{ji,x}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\psi}_{i}w_{ji,x}dx,

\Lambda_{j2}^{(i)}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\eta_{ji})\tilde{\phi}_{j}Z_{ji}dx-\sum_{k\not=j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{ki}\tilde{\phi}_{ki}Z_{ji}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\psi}_{i}Z_{ji}dx

and

\eta_{ji}=\eta\left(\frac{x-\beta(\varepsilon z)f_{ji}(\varepsilon z)}{R}\right).

Let
\bar{\phi}_{j}=\tilde{\phi}_{j1}-\tilde{\phi}_{j2}
. Then it satisfies the following equation

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{1}}-T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{2}}\right)\left\{\tilde{\chi}_{j1}h+p\left[|w_{j1}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j1}-1)-|\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j1}\tilde{\phi}_{j1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-p\left[|\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j1}\tilde{\psi}_{1},\Lambda_{j1}^{(1)},\Lambda_{j2}^{(1)}\right\}

\displaystyle+T_{j,\mathbf{f}_{2}}\left\{(\tilde{\chi}_{j1}-\tilde{\chi}_{j2})h-p[|\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1)-|\mathcal{V}^{(2)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(2)}-1)]\tilde{\chi}_{j2}\tilde{\psi}_{2}\right.

\displaystyle-p(\tilde{\chi}_{j1}-\tilde{\chi}_{j2})\left[|\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\psi}_{1}-p\left[|\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1)+1\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j2}(\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi_{2}})

\displaystyle+p\left[|w_{j1}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j1}-1)-|\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j1}\tilde{\phi}_{1}

\displaystyle\left.-p\left[|w_{j2}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j2}-1)-|\mathcal{V}^{(2)}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}^{(2)}-1)\right]\tilde{\chi}_{j2}\tilde{\phi}_{2},\Lambda_{j1}^{(1)}-\Lambda_{j1}^{(2)},\Lambda_{j2}^{(1)}-\Lambda_{j2}^{(2)}\right\}.

From (
5.30
), (
5.33
), Lemma
5.3
and direct computation, we get (
5.34
).

From the same method, we get

\|\tilde{\psi}(f_{1},e_{1})-\tilde{\psi}(f_{2},e_{2})\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

(5.35)

From (
5.34
), (
5.35
) and direct computation, we get (
5.37
).
∎

Now we consider the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{L}(\phi)=h+\sum_{j=1}^{N}c_{j}(\varepsilon z)w_{j,x}+\sum_{j=1}^{N}d_{j}(\varepsilon z)Z_{j},&\mbox{in $\mathfrak{S}$,}\\
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)w_{j,x}(x)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(z,x)Z_{j}(x)dx=0,&z\in(0,1/\varepsilon),\\
\phi(0,x)=\phi(1/\varepsilon,x),\quad\phi_{z}(0,x)=\phi_{z}(1/\varepsilon,x),&x\in\mathbb{R}.\end{array}\right.

(5.36)

Recall the operator
\mathcal{L}
is defined in (
4.12
). In the view of Proposition
5.4
, (
5.36
) is written into the following fixed point problem

\phi=\tilde{T}[-B_{5}(\phi)+h].

From the definition of
B_{5}(\phi)
, we get

\|B_{5}(\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\delta\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

Moreover,
B_{5}(\cdot)
is Lipschitz dependent on
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
:

\|B_{5,\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}}(\phi)-B_{5,\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2}}(\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{(}S))}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right]\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

With the help of Proposition
5.4
and fixed point theorem, we get the following proposition.

Proposition 5.5
.

Fix
\delta>0
small enough, (
5.36
) has a unique solution
\phi=T(h)
, where
T
is a linear operator satisfies the following estimate

\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(5.37)

Here
C>0
is a constant independent of
\varepsilon
and the choice of
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
.

Moreover,
T
is Lipschitz continously of
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
:

\|T_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}}-T_{\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2}}\|\leq C\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

(5.38)

Remark 5.6
.

Provided
\delta>0
small enough and the function
h
is supported in
|x|\leq 2\delta/\varepsilon
, the function
\phi=T(h)
satisfies the following estimate

|\phi(z,x)|+|\nabla\phi(z,x)|\leq C\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{3\varepsilon}}.

(5.39)

Proof.

According to the definition of
\mathcal{L}
, we get
\phi
satisfies an equation of the following form

\phi_{xx}+a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\phi_{zz}+(-p+o(1))\phi+O(\delta)(|D^{2}\phi|+\varepsilon|\nabla\phi|)=0,\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad|x|\geq\frac{7\delta}{3\varepsilon}.

Using the barrier function as the form

\varphi(z,x)=\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}}{2}(x-\frac{7\delta}{3\varepsilon})},

we get

|\phi(z,x)|\leq C\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}}{2}(x-\frac{7\delta}{3\varepsilon})}\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad|x|\geq\frac{7\delta}{3\varepsilon}.

Using local elliptic estimate, we get (
5.39
).
∎

6.
Nonlinear problem

In this section, we will solve (
4.14
)-(
4.16
) via Proposition
5.5
and Remark
5.6
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Fixed the constant
p>2
. There is a constant
D
such that for
\varepsilon>0
small enough and
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{e})
satisfying (
3.7
) and (
3.8
), (
4.14
)-(
4.16
) has a unique solution
\hat{\varphi}=\hat{\varphi}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{e})
, which satisfies

\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq D\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu}

and

\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|>3\delta/\varepsilon)}+\|\nabla\hat{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|>3\delta/\varepsilon)}\leq\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{4\varepsilon}}.

(6.1)

Besides,
\hat{\varphi}
depends Lipschitz-continuously on
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{e}
, i.e.

\|\hat{\varphi}_{\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}}-\hat{\varphi}_{\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2}}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

(6.2)

Proof.

With the help of Proposition
5.4
, (
4.14
)-(
4.15
) is written into the following fixed point problem

\phi=\mathcal{A}(\phi),

(6.3)

where

\mathcal{A}(\phi):=T\left\{-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{E}_{2}-\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\phi+\hat{\psi}(\phi))-p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[\left|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)+\left|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)\right]\hat{\psi}(\phi)\right\}.

Consider the following closed, bounded subset of
H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})
:

\mathfrak{B}=\left\{\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq D\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu},\quad\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|>3\delta/\varepsilon)}+\sup_{|x|\geq\frac{3\delta}{\varepsilon}-1}\|\nabla\phi\|_{L^{2}(B_{1}(z,x)}\leq\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{4\varepsilon}}\right\}.

Here
D>0
is a constant large enough, which we will claim later. We will show
\mathcal{A}
is a contraction map from
\mathfrak{B}
into itself.

From the definition of
\hat{N}(\phi)
, we get

|\hat{N}(\phi)|\leq C\left[|\phi|^{2}+|\phi|^{p}\right].

Let

\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi)=\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\phi+\hat{\psi}(\phi)).

Then we get

\|\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\left[\|\phi\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\|\phi\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S})}^{p}+\|\psi(\phi)\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}^{2}+\|\psi(\phi)\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}^{p}\right].

where
\mathfrak{S}_{\delta}=\mathfrak{S}\cap\{|x|\leq 2\delta/\varepsilon\}
. However, for the last two terms in the inequality above, we get

\|\psi(\phi)\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}^{2}+\|\psi(\phi)\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}^{p}\leq Ce^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{4\varepsilon}}\left[1+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{p}\right]+C\varepsilon^{-1}\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2\min\{p,2\}}.

For
\phi\in\mathfrak{B}
, we get

\|\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu})^{\min\{p,2\}}.

Now we estimate the Lipschitz property of
N_{1}
. From the definition of
\hat{N}(\phi)
we have

|\hat{N}^{\prime}(\phi)|\leq C|\phi|^{\min\{p-1,1\}}.

For
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\mathfrak{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi_{1})-\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle A\left[\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S})}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\|\psi(\phi_{1})-\psi(\phi_{2})\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S_{\delta}})}+\|\psi(\phi_{1})-\psi(\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S_{\delta}})}\right],

where
A=A_{1}+A_{2}
and

A_{l}=\|\phi_{l}\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S})}^{p-1}+\|\phi_{l}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\psi(\phi_{l})\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}^{p-1}+\|\psi(\phi_{l})\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})},\qquad\mathrm{for}\quad l=1,2.

Since

\displaystyle\|\psi(\phi_{l})\|_{L^{2p}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}^{p-1}+\|\psi(\phi_{l})\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{S}_{\delta}|^{1/2}\|\psi(\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}}+|\mathfrak{S}_{\delta}|^{\frac{p-1}{2p}}\|\psi(\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{4\varepsilon}}\left[1+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{p-1}\right]+\varepsilon^{-\frac{p-1}{2p}}\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{(p-1)\min\{p,2\}}+\varepsilon^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{\min\{p,2\}}.

We get

\|\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi_{1})-\tilde{N}_{1}(\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq\varepsilon^{\min\{p-1,1\}(\frac{3}{2}-\mu)}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.4)

Let

\tilde{N}_{2}(\phi)=p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[\left|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)+\left|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right|^{p-2}\left(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}\right)\right]\hat{\psi}(\phi).

It is apparent that

\|\tilde{N}_{2}(\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\left[e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{2\varepsilon}}+\varepsilon\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{4\varepsilon}}+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{\min\{p,2\}}\right]\leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{\min\{p,2\}}

and

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}_{2}(\phi_{1})-\tilde{N}_{2}(\phi_{2})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{-1/2}\left[\varepsilon\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{\sqrt{p}\delta}{4\varepsilon}}+(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu})^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{-1/2}(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu})^{\min\{p-1,1\}}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

Then for
\phi\in\mathfrak{B}
, we get

\|\mathcal{A}(\phi)\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C_{0}\varepsilon^{3/2}|\log\varepsilon|^{q}.

For
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we get

\|\mathcal{A}(\phi_{1})-\mathcal{A}(\phi_{2})\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

Then the fixed point problem (
6.3
) has a unique solution
\hat{\varphi}
satisfying
\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu}
. From Remark
5.6
and Sobolev embedding theorem, we get
\hat{\varphi}\in\mathfrak{B}
and (
6.1
) holds.

Now we estimate (
6.2
). It is easy to get

\partial_{f_{k}}\hat{N}(\phi)=p\left\{|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{q}}+\phi|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{q}}+\phi)-|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{q}})-(p-1)|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}\phi\right\}\partial_{f_{k}}\mathcal{V}.

Then

|\partial_{f_{k}}\hat{N}(\phi)|\leq C\left[|\phi|^{p-1}+|\phi|^{2}\right].

We can also get the similar estimate of
\partial_{e_{k}}\hat{N}(\phi)
. Using the similar method as in (
6.4
), we get

\displaystyle\|\tilde{N}_{1,\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}}-\tilde{N}_{1,\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu})^{\min\{p-1,2\}}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right]

(6.5)

\displaystyle+C\varepsilon^{-1}(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu})^{\min\{p,2\}\min\{p-1,2\}}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

From the same method and (
4.10
), we get

\|\tilde{N}_{2,\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}}-\tilde{N}_{2,\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1/2}(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}-\mu})^{\min\{p,2\}}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

(6.6)

Hence we get (
6.2
) from (
6.5
), (
6.6
), (
3.31
).
∎

From Proposition
6.1
, we know the solution
\hat{\varphi}
satisfies the assumption (
4.9
) before solving the outer problem (
4.4
).

7.
Reduced problem

To solve the inner problem, we only need to make the constants
c_{j}(\varepsilon z)
’s and
d_{j}(\varepsilon z)
’s in (
4.14
) equal to zero. We only need to solve the following problems

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})w_{j,x}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})+|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})\right]\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi})w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})w_{j,x}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\hat{\varphi}+\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi}))w_{j,x}dx=0

(7.1)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})Z_{j}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})+|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})\right]\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi})Z_{j}dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})Z_{j}dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\hat{\varphi}+\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi}))Z_{j}dx=0.

(7.2)

In this section, we consider the terms in (
7
) and (
7.3
) as the function of
\theta=\varepsilon z
.

We first consider the equation (
7
). From direct computation,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{11}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}_{zz}w_{j,x}dx+p\int_{R}\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|(\mathcal{V}-1)-|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)\right]\hat{\varphi}w_{j,x}dx

(7.3)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}p\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[|\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})-|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\right]\hat{\varphi}w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}(B_{4}^{1}(\hat{\varphi})+B_{4}^{3}(\hat{\varphi}))w_{j,x}dx.

From (
4.15
), we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}_{zz}w_{j,x}dx=2\varepsilon(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}_{z}w_{j,xx}dx+\varepsilon^{2}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}w_{j,xx}dx-\varepsilon^{2}|(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}w_{j,xxx}dx=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

Then

\|I_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon\|f_{j}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\left(\int_{0}^{1}d\theta\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|\hat{\varphi}_{z}(\theta/\varepsilon,x)\right|^{2}dx\right)^{1/2}\leq C\varepsilon^{3/2}\|f_{j}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}},

where
\mu_{1}\in(0,1/2)
is a constant.
From the same method, we get

\|I_{3}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}.

For the term
I_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|I_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{4}\int_{0}^{1}\left[|f_{j}|^{2}+|f_{j}^{\prime}|^{2}+|f_{j}^{\prime\prime}|^{2}\right]\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}(\theta/\varepsilon,x)w_{j,x}dx\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}\int_{0}^{1}\left[|f_{j}|^{2}+|f_{j}^{\prime}|^{2}+|f_{j}^{\prime\prime}|^{2}\right]\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{\varphi}(\theta/\varepsilon,x)|^{2}dx.

Let

F(\theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{\varphi}(\theta/\varepsilon,x)|^{2}dx.

From direct computation, we get

\|F\|_{W^{1,1}(0,1)}\leq C\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}.

Using Sobolev embedding theorem(
[
8
, Theorem 8.8]
), we get

\sup_{\theta\in(0,1)}|F(\theta)|\leq\|F\|_{W^{1,1}(0,1)}\leq C\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}.

Hence

\|I_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\|f_{j}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{H^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}.

Let

I_{7}=p\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)-|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)\right]\hat{\varphi}w_{j,x}dx.

Then we get

|I_{7}|\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\hat{\varphi}(\theta/\varepsilon,x)|^{2}dx\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)-|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)\right]^{2}w_{j,x}^{2}dx\right)^{1/2}.

Hence

\|I_{7}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{3/2}\|\hat{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}.

Let

I_{4}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}(B_{4}^{1}(\hat{\varphi})+B_{4}^{3}(\hat{\varphi}))w_{j,x}dx.

From the expression of
B_{4}^{1}
and
B_{4}^{3}
, we get

\|I_{4}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}.

Let

I_{5}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[|\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\eta_{3\delta}^{\varepsilon}\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})-|\mathcal{V}-1|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-1)\right]\hat{\varphi}w_{j,x}dx.

It is easy to get
\|I_{5}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}
.

In the expression of (
7.3
), we single out all the non-regular terms. The first term is

\tilde{I}_{2}=\varepsilon^{2}\beta f_{j}^{\prime\prime}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\varphi}(\theta/\varepsilon,z)w_{j,x}dx.

From (
6.2
) and direct computation, we have

\|\tilde{I}_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{1})-\tilde{I}_{2}(\mathbf{e}_{2},\mathbf{f}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{3}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

However, the other non-regular terms in (
7.3
) come from the terms containing the
\hat{\varphi}_{zz}
in
B_{4}^{1}(\phi)
and
B_{4}^{3}(\phi)
. That is

\tilde{I}_{4}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{6\delta}^{\varepsilon}\tilde{\alpha}^{1-p}(X_{1}-a_{11})\hat{\varphi}_{zz}w_{j,x}dx,

where
X_{1}
is defined in Lemma
2.2
.
It is easy to get

\|\tilde{I}_{4}(\mathbf{e}_{1},\mathbf{f}_{1})-\tilde{I}_{4}(\mathbf{e}_{2},\mathbf{f}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right].

From the same method in
[
19
,
42
]
, we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{L}(\hat{\varphi})w_{j,x}dx-\tilde{I}_{2}-\tilde{I}_{4}

is a compact operator.

For the other terms in (
7
), we define

\Lambda_{1}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\left[|\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\mathcal{V}-\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})+|\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}}|^{p-2}(\tilde{\alpha}^{-1}\bar{\mathbf{q}})\right]\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi})w_{j,x}dx

and

\Lambda_{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}\hat{N}(\hat{\varphi}+\hat{\psi}(\hat{\varphi}))w_{j,x}dx.

From direct computation, we get

\|\Lambda_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\|\psi(\hat{\varphi})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\varepsilon^{3/2-\mu})^{\min\{p,2\}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}

and

\|\Lambda_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}(\varepsilon^{3/2-\mu})^{\min\{p,2\}}+(\varepsilon^{3/2-\mu})^{2\min\{p,2\}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}.

From (
C.6
), we rewrite (
7
) into the following form

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\alpha^{1-p}\beta\left\{-a_{11}f_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\left[a_{22}-b_{11}-a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)\right]f_{j}^{\prime}+\left[a_{22}(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime})+b_{22}-a_{33}\right]f_{j}\right.

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}\right]f_{j}

\displaystyle\left.+\left[-2\varepsilon a_{11}e_{j}^{\prime}f_{j}^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}e_{j}^{\prime}f_{j}\right]\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx\right)^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx\right\}

\displaystyle C_{0}p\alpha_{p}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]=\varepsilon^{2}M_{1j\varepsilon}.

(7.4)

Using the same procedure and (
C.7
), we get (
7
) is written into the following form

\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{j})+p\alpha_{p}C_{1}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}+e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]=\varepsilon^{2}M_{2j\varepsilon}.

(7.5)

In the expression (
7
) and (
7.5
),
M_{ij\varepsilon}=A_{ij\varepsilon}+K_{ij\varepsilon}
,
i=1,2
, where
K_{ij\varepsilon}
is a compact operator and
A_{ij\varepsilon}
is a Lipschitz operator. It satisfies the following estimates

\|A_{ij\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1})-A_{ij\varepsilon}(\mathbf{f}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}\left[\|\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right]

and

\|A_{ij\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}},\qquad\|K_{ij\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}.

(7.6)

8.
Proof of Theorem

To prove our main theorem, we only need to find a solution to (
7
)-(
7.5
). To do job, we need some priori estimate.

Let

\breve{f}_{j}(\theta)=\beta(\theta)f_{j}(\theta).

(8.1)

Then we have

f_{j}=\beta^{-1}\breve{f}_{j},\qquad f_{j}^{\prime}=\beta^{-1}\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime}-\beta^{-2}\beta^{\prime}\breve{f}_{j}

and

f_{j}^{\prime\prime}=\beta^{-1}\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime\prime}-2\beta^{-2}\beta^{\prime}\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime}+\left[2\beta^{-3}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}-\beta^{-2}\beta^{\prime\prime}\right]\breve{f}_{j}.

Hence (
7
) is transformed into the following problem

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\alpha^{1-p}\left\{-a_{11}\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\left[a_{22}-b_{11}+a_{11}(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}-2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime})\right]\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime}-2\varepsilon a_{11}e_{j}^{\prime}\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}\right.

\displaystyle+\left[2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}a_{22}+\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}b_{11}+b_{22}-a_{33}+\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}+a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}-\beta^{-2}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}\right)\right]\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime}

(8.2)

\displaystyle\left.+\left(2\varepsilon a_{11}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}e_{j}^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}e_{j}^{\prime}\right)\breve{f}_{j}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}\right\}+p\alpha_{p}C_{0}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(\breve{f}_{j}-\breve{f}_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}(\breve{f}_{j+1}-\breve{f}_{j})}\right]=\varepsilon^{2}M_{1j\varepsilon}.

For notation simplicity, we denote

\Upsilon_{2}=\alpha^{1-p}a_{11},\qquad\Upsilon_{1}=\alpha^{1-p}\left[a_{22}-b_{11}+a_{11}(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}-2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime})\right],

(8.3)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\alpha^{1-p}\left[2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}a_{22}+\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}b_{11}+b_{22}-a_{33}+\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}\right.

(8.4)

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}+a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}-\beta^{-2}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}\right)\right]

\Upsilon_{1j}(\mathbf{e})=\Upsilon_{1}-2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}a_{11}e_{j}^{\prime}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}

and

\Upsilon_{0j}(\mathbf{e})=\Upsilon_{0}-\alpha^{1-p}\left(2\varepsilon a_{11}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}e_{j}^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}e_{j}^{\prime}\right)\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}.

Then (
8
) is written into the following form

\varepsilon^{2}\left(-\Upsilon_{2}\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1j}(\mathbf{e})\breve{f}_{j}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0j}(\mathbf{e})\breve{f}_{j}\right)+p\alpha_{p}C_{0}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(\breve{f}_{j}-\breve{f}_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}(\breve{f}_{j+1}-\breve{f}_{j})}\right]=\varepsilon^{2}M_{1j\varepsilon}.

Let

\varepsilon^{2}\rho_{\varepsilon}=p\alpha_{p}C_{0}e^{-\sqrt{p}\rho_{\varepsilon}}.

(8.5)

Then we get

\rho_{\varepsilon}=\frac{2}{\sqrt{p}}|\log\varepsilon|-\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\log\left[\frac{2}{\sqrt{p}}|\log\varepsilon|\right]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\log(p\alpha_{p}C_{0})+O\left(\frac{\log|\log\varepsilon|}{|\log\varepsilon|}\right).

(8.6)

Denote
\sigma=\rho_{\varepsilon}^{-1}
and

\breve{f}_{j}(\theta)=\left(j-\frac{N+1}{2}\right)\rho_{\varepsilon}+d_{j}(\theta),\qquad j=1,\cdots,N,

Then
d_{j}
’s satisfy the following equations

\tilde{R}_{j}(\mathbf{d})=\sigma\left(-\Upsilon_{2}d_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1j}(\mathbf{e})d_{j}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0j}(\mathbf{e})\breve{f}_{j}\right)+\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j}-d_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j+1}-d_{j})}\right]=\sigma M_{1j\varepsilon},

(8.7)

where
j=1,2,\cdots,N
and
\mathbf{d}=(d_{1},d_{2},\cdots,d_{N})^{t}
.

Using the notation (
8.1
) and (
8.5
), we rewrite (
7.5
) into the following problem

\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{j}+\varepsilon C_{1}C_{0}^{-1}\rho_{\varepsilon}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j}-d_{j-1})}+e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j+1}-d_{j})}\right]=\varepsilon M_{2j\varepsilon}.

(8.8)

To solve (
7
)-(
7.5
), we only need to find a solution to the problem (
8.7
)-(
8.8
).

Let

\mathbf{e}=\begin{bmatrix}e_{1}\\
e_{2}\\
\vdots\\
e_{N}\end{bmatrix},\qquad\tilde{\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{d})=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{R}_{1}(\mathbf{d})\\
\tilde{R}_{2}(\mathbf{d})\\
\vdots\\
\tilde{R}_{N}(\mathbf{d})\end{bmatrix},\qquad\mathbf{M}_{i\varepsilon}=\begin{bmatrix}M_{i1\varepsilon}\\
M_{i2\varepsilon}\\
\vdots\\
M_{iN\varepsilon}\end{bmatrix},\quad\mathrm{for}\quad i=1,2,

and

\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{d})=\varepsilon C_{1}C_{0}^{-1}\rho_{\varepsilon}\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{2}-d_{1})}\\
e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{2}-d_{1})}+e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{3}-d_{2})}\\
\vdots\\
e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{N-1}-d_{N-2})}+e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{N}-d_{N-1})}\\
e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{N}-d_{N-1})}\end{bmatrix}.

Then (
7
)-(
7.5
) is written into the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{d})=\sigma\mathbf{M}_{1\varepsilon},\\
\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\mathbf{e}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}\mathbf{e}+\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{d})=\varepsilon\mathbf{M}_{2\varepsilon}.\end{array}\right.

(8.9)

It is apparent that
\tilde{R}_{j}(\mathbf{d})=R_{j}(\mathbf{d})-P_{j}(e_{j},d_{j})
, where

R_{j}(\mathbf{d}):=\sigma\left(-\Upsilon_{2}d_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}d_{j}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\breve{f}_{j}\right)+\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j}-d_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j+1}-d_{j})}\right]

and

P_{j}(e_{j},d_{j}):=2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}a_{11}e_{j}^{\prime}d_{j}^{\prime}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}-2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}\left[a_{11}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+a_{22}\right]e_{j}^{\prime}\breve{f}_{j}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}.

Denote
\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{d})=(R_{1}(\mathbf{d}),\cdots,R_{N}(\mathbf{d}))^{t}
. Let

v_{j}=d_{j+1}-d_{j},\qquad j=1,\cdots,N-1,\quad\mathrm{and}\quad v_{N}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}d_{j}.

Then

d_{j}=\frac{1}{N}v_{N}-\sum_{k=j}^{N-1}v_{k}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k},\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad j=1,2,\cdots,N.

(8.10)

From direct computation, we have

Q_{N}(v_{N}):=\sum_{j=1}^{N}R_{j}(\mathbf{d})=\sigma\left[-\Upsilon_{2}v_{N}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}v_{N}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}v_{N}\right]

and

\displaystyle Q_{j}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{j+1}(\mathbf{d})-R_{j}(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma\left(-\Upsilon_{2}v_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}v_{j}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}(\rho_{\varepsilon}+v_{j})\right)

\displaystyle+\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{2}}+2e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{1}},&j=1,\\
-e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{j+1}}+2e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{j}}-e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{j-1}},&j=2,\cdots,N-1,\\
2e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{N-1}}-e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{N-2}},&j=N-1.\end{array}\right.

Denote

\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{v})=\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})\\
Q_{N}(v_{N})\end{bmatrix},\qquad\bar{Q}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})=\begin{bmatrix}Q_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})\\
\vdots\\
Q_{N-1}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})\end{bmatrix}.

Then we get

\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{v})=B\mathbf{R}(B^{-1}(\mathbf{v})),

where

B=\begin{bmatrix}-1&1&&&\\
0&-1&1&&\\
&&\ddots&\ddots\\
&&&-1&1\\
1&1&\cdots&1&1\end{bmatrix}.

Then we get
\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{v})=0
is equivalent to
\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{d})=0
.

8.1.
Approximate solution

To solve the first equation in (
8.9
), we consider the following problem first

R_{j}(\mathbf{d}):=\sigma\left(-\Upsilon_{2}d_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}d_{j}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\breve{f}_{j}\right)+\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j}-d_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}(d_{j+1}-d_{j})}\right]=0,

(8.12)

where
j=1,2,\cdots,N
. In this subsection, we want to construct an approximate solution to (
8.12
). According to the argument above, we consider the equation
\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{v})=0
instead.

From Remark
2.4
, we get
Q_{N}(v_{N})=0
has the only trivial solution
v_{N}=0
. However, we have

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})=\sigma\left[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{v}}\right]-\Upsilon_{0}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\\
\vdots\\
1\end{bmatrix}+\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})=0,

(8.13)

where

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}})=M\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{1}}\\
\vdots\\
e^{-\sqrt{p}v_{N-1}}\end{bmatrix},\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad M=\begin{bmatrix}2&-1&&&\\
-1&2&-1&&\\
&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&\\
&&-1&2&-1\\
&&&-1&2\end{bmatrix}.

Now we construct an approximate solution of (
8.13
).

Proposition 8.1
.

For any integer
k\geq 1
, there exists a function
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}(y,\sigma)=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\sigma\eta_{k}(y,\sigma)
such that
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})=O(\sigma^{k})
, where
\eta_{1}\equiv 0
,
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\log\left[\frac{\Upsilon_{0}}{2}(N-i)i\right]
and
\eta_{k}
is continuous on
\Gamma\times[0,+\infty)
.

Let

\mathbf{h}_{k}=B^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}\\
0\end{bmatrix}.

(8.14)

There holds that
\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{h}_{k})=O(\sigma^{k})
.

Proof.

Let
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}=(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{11},\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{12},\cdots,\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1(N-1)})^{t}
be the unique solution of the following linear problem

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})=M\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{11}}\\
e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{12}}\\
\vdots\\
e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1(N-1)}}\end{bmatrix}=\Upsilon_{0}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\\
\vdots\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

In fact,

\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1i}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\log\left(\frac{\Upsilon_{0}}{2}(N-i)i\right),\qquad i=1,\cdots,N-1.

(8.15)

Then we get

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}]=O(\sigma).

Hence

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\mathbf{w})=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}]+\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}]+D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})\mathbf{w}+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}),

(8.16)

where

\displaystyle D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{p}M\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{11}},&&&\\
&e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{12}}&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1(N-1)}}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{p}}{2}\Upsilon_{0}\begin{bmatrix}2r_{1}&-r_{2}&&&&\\
-r_{1}&2r_{2}&-r_{3}&&&\\
&-r_{2}&\ddots&\ddots&&\\
&&\ddots&&&\\
&&&-r_{N-3}&2r_{N-2}&-r_{N-1}\\
&&&&-r_{N-2}&2r_{N-1}\end{bmatrix},

r_{i}=(N-i)i
and

N_{1}(\mathbf{w})=\frac{\Upsilon_{0}}{2}M\begin{bmatrix}r_{1}\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}w_{1}}-1+\sqrt{p}w_{1}\right)\\
r_{2}\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}w_{2}}-1+\sqrt{p}w_{2}\right)\\
\vdots\\
r_{N-1}\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}w_{N-1}}-1+\sqrt{p}w_{N-1}\right)\end{bmatrix}\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathbf{w}=\begin{bmatrix}w_{1}\\
w_{2}\\
\vdots\\
w_{N-1}\end{bmatrix}.

Let
\mathbf{w}_{1}=O(\sigma)
be unique solution of the following problem

-D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})\mathbf{w}_{1}=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}].

Then the function
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{2}:=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\mathbf{w}_{1}
satisfies

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{2})=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}_{1}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1})=O(\sigma^{2})

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{2}+\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\mathbf{w}_{1}+\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}_{1}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1})+D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})\mathbf{w}

\displaystyle+\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\mathbf{w})-N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}).

Let
\mathbf{w}_{2}=O(\sigma^{2})
be the unique solution of the following equation

-D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})\mathbf{w}_{2}=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}_{1}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}).

Then the function
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{3}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{2}+\mathbf{w}_{2}
satisfies

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{3})=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}_{2}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}_{2}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}_{2}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\mathbf{w}_{2})-N(\mathbf{w}_{1}).

Assume for
k\geq 3
, the function
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k-1}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\mathbf{w}_{j}
satisfies

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k-1})=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}_{k-1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}_{k-1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}_{k-1}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k-1})-N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k-2})

and
\mathbf{w}_{j}=O(\sigma^{j})
,
j=1,\cdots,k-1
.

Let
\mathbf{w}_{k}=O(\sigma^{k})
be the unique solution of the following problem

-D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})\mathbf{w}_{k}=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}_{k-1}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}_{k-1}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}_{k-1}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k-1})-N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k-2})

and
\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k-1}+\mathbf{w}_{k}
.
From (
8.16
), we get

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k-1}+\mathbf{w}_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k-1})+\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{k}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{k}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{k}]+D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1})\mathbf{w}_{k}

\displaystyle+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k})-N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{k}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{k}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{k}]+N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k})-N_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1}+\cdots+\mathbf{w}_{k-1}).

Hence
\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})=O(\sigma^{k+1})
. This proposition follows.

∎

From (
8.10
), we get

\displaystyle S_{j}(\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle P_{j+1}(e_{j+1},d_{j+1})-P_{j}(e_{j},d_{j})

(8.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}a_{11}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}\left[e_{j+1}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{N}v_{N}^{\prime}-\sum_{k=j+1}^{N-1}v_{k}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k}^{\prime}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-e_{j}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{N}v_{N}^{\prime}-\sum_{k=j}^{N-1}v_{k}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k}^{\prime}\right)\right]-2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}[a_{11}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+a_{22}]\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}\times

\displaystyle\left\{e_{j+1}^{\prime}\left[(j-\frac{N-1}{2})\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{N}v_{N}-\sum_{k=j+1}^{N-1}v_{k}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-e_{j}^{\prime}\left[(j-\frac{N+1}{2})\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{N}v_{N}-\sum_{k=j}^{N-1}v_{k}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k}\right]\right\},

and

\displaystyle S_{N}(\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{N}P_{j}(e_{j},d_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}a_{11}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx}{\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx}\sum_{j=1}^{N}e_{j}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{N}v_{N}^{\prime}-\sum_{k=j}^{N-1}v_{k}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon\alpha^{1-p}\left[a_{11}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+a_{22}\right]\sum_{j=1}^{N}e_{j}^{\prime}\left[(j-\frac{N+1}{2})\rho_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{N}v_{N}-\sum_{k=j}^{N-1}v_{k}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}kv_{k}\right].

Denote

\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{v})=\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\mathbf{v})\\
S_{N}(\mathbf{v})\end{bmatrix},\qquad\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\mathbf{v})=\begin{bmatrix}S_{1}(\mathbf{v})\\
S_{2}(\mathbf{v})\\
\vdots\\
S_{N-1}(\mathbf{v}))\end{bmatrix}.

(8.18)

8.2.
Related problems

Now we consider the problem

\tilde{\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{d})=\mathbf{g}.

(8.19)

From the calculation above, we see it is equivalent to the following problem

\mathbf{Q}(\mathbf{u})=\bar{\mathbf{g}}+\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{u}),

where
\bar{\mathbf{g}}=B\mathbf{g}
.
It is equivalent to the following system

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{u}_{N}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{u}_{N}]=g_{N}+S_{N}(\mathbf{u}),

(8.20)

\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{u}})=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\bar{\mathbf{u}}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\bar{\mathbf{u}}]-\Upsilon_{0}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
\vdots\\
1\end{bmatrix}+\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{u}})=\bar{\mathbf{g}}+\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\mathbf{u}),

(8.21)

where

\mathbf{u}=\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{u}}\\
u_{N}\end{bmatrix},\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\mathbf{g}=\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{g}}\\
g_{N}\end{bmatrix}.

To solve problem (
8.20
)-(
8.21
), we first consider (
8.21
) for given
u_{N}
. We consider the solution
\bar{\mathbf{u}}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}
of (
8.21
). It is equivalent to solving the following equation

\mathbf{J}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{w}):=\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\mathbf{w}^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\mathbf{w}]+D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})\mathbf{w}=\bar{\mathbf{g}}+\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w})-N_{2}(\mathbf{w})-\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}),

(8.22)

where

N_{2}(\mathbf{w})=\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w})-\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})-D\bar{\mathbf{Q}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})\mathbf{w}.

To solve this problem, we consider the following linear problem

\mathbf{J}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{w})=\tilde{\mathbf{g}},\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad(0,1).

(8.23)

Lemma 8.2
.

For
\varepsilon>0
satisfying the condition

\left|\frac{2}{\sqrt{p}}\Lambda_{i}|\log\varepsilon|-\frac{4\pi^{2}j^{2}}{l_{1}^{2}}\right|>c_{1}\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{p}|log\varepsilon|}\right)^{1/2},\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad i=1,\cdots,N,

(8.24)

small enough, (
8.23
) has a unique solution
\mathbf{w}=F(\tilde{\mathbf{g}})
provided
\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\in L^{2}(0,1)
. Moreover,
\mathbf{w}
satisfies the following estimate

\sigma\|\mathbf{w}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{w}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Proof.

Let
\phi=M^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{g}_{0}=M^{-\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\mathbf{g}}
. Then (
8.23
) is written into the following problem

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}-\Upsilon_{0}\phi]-\sqrt{p}M^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k(N-1)}}\end{bmatrix}M^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi=\mathbf{g}_{0},

where
(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k1},\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k2},\cdots,\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k(N-1)})^{t}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}
.
We rewrite the equation above into the following form

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}]-\mathbf{C}(y,\sigma)\phi=\mathbf{g}_{0},

(8.25)

where

C(y,\sigma)=\sigma\Upsilon_{0}I_{N-1}+\sqrt{p}M^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k1}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k(N-1)}}\end{bmatrix}M^{\frac{1}{2}}.

To solve problem (
8.25
), we consider the following problem

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}]-\mathbf{C}(y,0)\phi=\mathbf{g}.

(8.26)

From Proposition
8.1
and (
8.15
), we get

C(y,0)=\sqrt{p}M^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{11}}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&e^{-\sqrt{p}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{1(N-1)}}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{\sqrt{p}}{2}\Upsilon_{0}(y)M^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}r_{1}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&r_{N-1}\end{bmatrix}M^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Let
\mathbf{g}=(g_{1},g_{2},\cdots,g_{N})
,

\mathbf{D}=\frac{\sqrt{p}}{2}M^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}r_{1}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&r_{N-1}\end{bmatrix}M^{\frac{1}{2}},

and
\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{N-1}
be the eigenvalue of the matrix
\mathbf{D}
. Then (
8.26
) is equivalent to the following system:

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}]-\Lambda_{i}\Upsilon_{0}\phi=g_{i},\qquad i=1,\cdots,N-1.

(8.27)

For simplicity, we write this problem into the generic form

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}]-\mu\Upsilon_{0}\phi=g.

(8.28)

It is well known that (
8.28
) has a unique solution provided that
\mu/\sigma
differs from all the eigenvalues
\lambda=\tilde{\lambda}_{j}
of the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}=\lambda\Upsilon_{0}\phi,\qquad\mathrm{in}\quad(0,1),\\
\phi(0)=\phi(1),\quad\phi^{\prime}(0)=\phi^{\prime}(1).\end{array}\right.

(8.29)

Moreover, its solution has the following estimate

\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq\frac{C\sigma^{-1}}{\min_{j}\left|\mu/\sigma-\tilde{\lambda}_{j}\right|}\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Using the following Liouville transformation

l_{1}=\int_{0}^{1}\sqrt{\frac{\Upsilon_{0}(t)}{\Upsilon_{2}(t)}}dt,\quad s=\frac{\pi}{l_{1}}\int_{0}^{t}\sqrt{\frac{\Upsilon_{0}(\theta)}{\Upsilon_{2}(\theta)}}d\theta,\quad\psi(s)=\Phi(t)\phi(t),

\Phi(t)=\sqrt[4]{\frac{\Upsilon_{0}(t)}{\Upsilon_{2}(t)}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\Upsilon_{1}(t)}{\Upsilon_{2}(t)}dt\right),

(
8.29
) is written into the following form

\psi^{\prime\prime}+q(s)\psi+\frac{\lambda l_{1}^{2}}{\pi^{2}}\psi=0,\qquad\mathrm{in}\quad(0,\pi),

where
q(s)
is a smooth function. From
[
29
]
, we get the following estimate

\tilde{\lambda}_{j}=\frac{4\pi^{2}j^{2}}{l_{1}^{2}}+o(j^{-2})\qquad\mathrm{as}\qquad j\to\infty.

From (
8.6
) and the condition (
8.24
), we get
problem (
8.27
) has a unique solution

\phi=S(g)=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\cdots,\phi_{N-1})^{t},

which satisfies the following estimate

\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

(8.30)

Now we estimate the derivatives of
\phi
. Multiplying both sides of (
8.28
) by
\phi
and integrating, we have

\sigma\int_{0}^{1}\left[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}\right]\phi dt-\mu\int_{0}^{1}\Upsilon_{0}\phi^{2}dt=\int_{0}^{1}g\phi dt.

Then we get

\sigma\int_{0}^{1}\Upsilon_{2}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}dt+\sigma\int_{0}^{1}(\Upsilon_{1}+\Upsilon_{2}^{\prime})\phi\phi^{\prime}dt=\mu\int_{0}^{1}\Upsilon_{0}\phi^{2}dt+\int_{0}^{1}g\phi dt.

Hence

\sigma\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}\leq C\sigma\int_{0}^{1}\Upsilon_{2}|\phi^{\prime}|^{2}dt\leq C\left[\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}+\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\right].

From (
8.30
) we get

\sigma\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}\leq C\sigma^{-1}\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}.

That is

\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

(8.31)

From (
8.28
), (
8.30
) and (
8.31
), we get (
8.28
) has a unique solution,
\phi
satisfying the following estimate

\sigma\|\phi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

We write (
8.25
) into the following form

\sigma[-\Upsilon_{2}\phi^{\prime\prime}+\Upsilon_{1}\phi^{\prime}]-C(y,0)\phi=\mathbf{g}_{0}+\left[C(y,\sigma)-C(y,0)\right]\phi.

Using fixed point theorem, we get (
8.25
) has a unique solution satisfying the estimate

\sigma\|\phi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{g}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Hence this lemma follows.

∎

For the function
\phi\in H^{2}(0,1)
, we define the norm

\|\phi\|_{b}=\sigma\|\phi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Recall
\mathbf{h}_{k}
is defined in (
8.14
). Then we get the following lemma.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
k>2
and
\varepsilon>0
satisfies (
8.24
). For all the functions
\mathbf{g}
with
\|\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq\sigma^{k}
, (
8.19
) has a unique solution of the form

\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{h}_{k}+H(\mathbf{g}),

where
H(\cdot)
satisfies

\|H(\mathbf{g})\|_{b}\leq C\sigma^{\frac{k+1}{2}}

and

\|H(\mathbf{g}_{1})-H(\mathbf{g}_{2})\|_{b}\leq C\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathbf{g}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}

Proof.

Recall that (
8.19
) is equivalent to (
8.20
) and (
8.21
). We first consider the solution
\bar{\mathbf{u}}=\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}
. With the help of Lemma
8.2
, (
8.21
) is written into the following fixed point problem

\mathbf{w}=F[\bar{\mathbf{g}}+\bar{S}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w})-N_{2}(\mathbf{w})-\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})]=\tilde{T}_{1}(\mathbf{w}).

(8.32)

Let

\mathcal{D}=\left\{\mathbf{w}\in H^{2}(0,1)\;:\;\|\mathbf{w}\|_{b}\leq\mu\sigma^{\frac{k+1}{2}}\right\}.
