We solve the fixed point problem (
8.21
) in
\mathcal{D}
.

From Proposition
8.1
, we get
\|\bar{\mathbf{Q}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{k}
. Using the definition of
\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\cdot)
in (
8.18
) and (
8.17
), we get

\displaystyle\|\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{3/4}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\|e_{j}^{\prime}\|\left(C+\|u_{N}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{1/4}\left(C+\|u_{N}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{b}\right).

From the definition of
N_{2}
, we get
|N_{2}(\mathbf{w})|\leq C|\mathbf{w}|^{2}
. Hence

\|N_{2}(\mathbf{w})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{L^{4}(0,1)}^{2}\leq C\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{b}^{2}.

For
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}(\mathbf{w})\|_{b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1/2}\left[\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon^{1/4}\left(C+\|u_{N}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{b}\right)+\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{w}\|^{2}+\sigma^{k}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1/2}\left(\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{k}+\varepsilon^{1/4}\|u_{N}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\right).

Then
\tilde{T}(\mathbf{w})\in\mathcal{D}
for
\mu
large enough.

However, for
\mathbf{w}_{1},\mathbf{w}_{2}\in\mathcal{D}
, we get
|N_{2}(\mathbf{w}_{1})-N_{2}(\mathbf{w}_{2})|\leq C\sigma^{\frac{k}{2}}|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}|
. Hence

\|N_{2}(\mathbf{w}_{1})-N_{2}(\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

From (
8.17
), we get

\|\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{1})-\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,2)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/4}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}.

Then

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{1})-\tilde{T}_{1}(\mathbf{w}_{2})\|_{b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1/2}\left(\sigma^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}+\varepsilon^{1/4}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{\frac{k-1}{2}}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}.

From fixed point theorem, we get (
8.21
) has a unique solution, we denote by
\mathbf{w}=\tilde{\Omega}(\bar{\mathbf{g}},u_{N})
.

Now we estimate dependence of
\tilde{\Omega}(\cdot,\cdot)
on its parameters. Let
\mathbf{w}_{i}=\tilde{\Omega}(\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{i},u_{Ni})
, where
i=1,2
. Then we get

\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}=F[\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{2}+\bar{S}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{1},u_{N1})-\bar{S}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{2},u_{N2})-(N_{2}(\mathbf{w}_{1}))-N_{2}(\bar{\mathbf{w}}_{2})].

From (
8.17
), we get

\|\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{1},u_{N1})-\bar{\mathbf{S}}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}_{2},u_{N2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2}\left(\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\right).

Then we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1/2}\left[\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{\frac{k}{2}}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\varepsilon^{1/2}\left(\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}+\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\right)\right].

Hence

\|\tilde{\Omega}(\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{1},u_{N1})-\tilde{\Omega}(\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{2},u_{N2})\|_{b}\leq C\left[\sigma^{-1/2}\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon^{1/4}\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\right].

(8.33)

After solving the problem (
8.21
), we consider (
8.20
). From fixed point theorem, we get (
8.20
) has a unique solution
u_{N}=\tilde{T}_{2}(g_{N})
satisfying

\|u_{N}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-1}\left[\|g_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon^{1/4}\right].

Let
u_{Ni}=\tilde{T}_{2}(g_{Ni})
,
i=1,2
. Using (
8.33
), we get

\displaystyle\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1}\left[\|g_{N1}-g_{N2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon^{1/2}\left(\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{w}_{1}-\mathbf{w}_{2}\|_{b}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1}\left[\|g_{N1}-g_{N2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon^{1/2}\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\right]+C\varepsilon^{1/4}\|\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Hence

\|u_{N1}-u_{N2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathbf{g}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Therefore (
8.24
) has a unique solution of the form

\mathbf{d}=B^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{k}+\mathbf{w}\\
u_{N}\end{bmatrix}.

This lemma follows.
∎

Now we consider the second equation in (
8.9
). We first consider the following problem

\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e=h,\quad\mathrm{in}\quad(0,1).

(8.34)

From the same method in
[
19
, Lemma 8.1]
, we get the following lemma.

Lemma 8.4
.

Assume

|\varepsilon^{2}k^{2}-\lambda_{*}|>c_{2}\varepsilon\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad\forall k\in Z_{+},

(8.35)

where

\lambda_{*}=\lambda_{0}l_{2}^{2}/4\pi\quad\mathrm{and}\quad l_{2}=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Upsilon_{2}(t)}}dt.

If
h\in L^{2}(0,1)
, (
8.34
) has unique solution
e=G(h)
, which satisfies

\varepsilon^{2}\|e^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon\|e^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\|e\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\|h\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

However, if
d\in H^{2}(0,1)
, we have

\varepsilon^{2}\|e^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\varepsilon\|e^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+\|e\|_{L^{\infty}(0,1)}\leq C\|h\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}.

(8.36)

8.3.
Solving (
8.9
)

From now on, we impose the condition

\|\mathbf{d}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}\leq M_{1},

(8.37)

where
M_{1}>0
is a constant large enough.
Let

\mathbf{e}_{0}(\mathbf{d})=G(\mathbf{B}(\mathbf{d})).

According to the estimate (
8.36
) and the condition (
8.37
), we get
\|\mathbf{e}_{0}\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon|\log\varepsilon|
and

\|\mathbf{e}_{0}(\mathbf{d}_{1})-\mathbf{e}_{0}(\mathbf{d}_{2})\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon|\log\varepsilon|\|\mathbf{d}_{1}-\mathbf{d}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}.

Let
\mathbf{e}=\mathbf{e}_{0}+\tilde{\mathbf{e}}
. Then (
8.9
) is transformed into the following equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{d})=\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{M}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}),\\
\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\tilde{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{e}}=\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{M}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}).\end{array}\right.

(8.38)

where
\tilde{\mathbf{M}}_{i\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{i\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})+\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{i\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})
,
i=1,2
. Here
\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{i\varepsilon}
is a Lipschitz operator satisfying

\|\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{i\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1})-\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{i\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2})\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}\left[\|\mathbf{d}_{1}-\mathbf{d}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right],

and
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{i\varepsilon}
is a compact operator. There also hold that

\|\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{i\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}},\qquad\|\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{i\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}.

Then we consider the following system

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{\mathbf{R}}(\mathbf{d})-\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\\
\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\tilde{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}-\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=\tilde{\mathbf{g}}.\end{array}\right.

(8.39)

Lemma 8.5
.

Assume the small constant
\varepsilon>0
satisfy (
8.24
) and (
8.35
).
Under the condition
\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}
and
\|\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+\mu_{1}}
, with
\mu_{1}\in(0,1/2)
, (
8.39
) has a unique solution
(\mathbf{d},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathcal{R}_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{g}}),\mathcal{R}_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{g}}))
, which satisfies

\|\mathcal{R}_{1}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{g}})\|_{b}\leq C\sigma^{\frac{k+1}{2}},\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\|\mathcal{R}_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\tilde{\mathbf{g}})\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon^{1/2+\mu_{1}}.

Moreover

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathbf{h}_{1},\mathbf{g}_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathbf{h}_{2},\mathbf{g}_{2})\|_{b}+\|\mathcal{R}_{2}(\mathbf{h}_{1},\mathbf{g}_{1})-\mathcal{R}_{1}(\mathbf{h}_{2},\mathbf{g}_{2})\|_{*}

(8.40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma^{-1}\|\mathbf{h}_{1}-\mathbf{h}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}+C\varepsilon^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{1}-\mathbf{g}_{2}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}.

Proof.

Substitute
\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathbf{p}
into (
8.39
). With the help of Lemma
8.3
and Lemma
8.4
, we only need to solve the following fixed point problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbf{p}=H\left[\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathbf{p},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})+\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\right],\\
\tilde{\mathbf{e}}=G\left[\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathbf{p},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})+\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\right].\end{array}\right.

(8.41)

Define

\mathcal{D}_{1}=\left\{(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e})\;:\;\|\mathbf{p}\|_{b}\leq\kappa\sigma^{\frac{k+1}{2}},\;\|\mathbf{e}\|_{*}\leq\kappa_{1}\varepsilon^{1/2+\mu_{1}}\right\}.

Let

\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\mathbf{p},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=H\left[\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathbf{p},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})+\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\right]

and

\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{p},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=G\left[\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{A}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathbf{p},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})+\tilde{\mathbf{g}}\right].

For
(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e})\in\mathcal{D}_{1}
, we get

\|\sigma\mathbf{A}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e})+\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\sigma\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}+\|\tilde{\mathbf{h}}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq\sigma^{k}.

From Lemma
8.3
, we get
\|\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e})\|_{b}\leq C\sigma^{\frac{k+1}{2}}
. However

\|\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e})\|_{*}\leq C\varepsilon^{-1}\left(\varepsilon^{\frac{3}{2}+\mu_{1}}+\|\mathbf{g}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}.

Hence
(\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e}),\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e}))\in\mathcal{D}_{1}
for
\kappa>0
and
\kappa_{1}>0
large enough.

For
(\mathbf{p}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1}),(\mathbf{p}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2})\in\mathcal{D}_{1}
, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\mathbf{p}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1})-\mathcal{H}(\tilde{\mathbf{h}},\mathbf{p}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2})\|_{b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}\left(\|\mathbf{p}_{1}-\mathbf{p}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|\mathbf{p}_{1}-\mathbf{p}_{2}\|_{b}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right),

and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{p}_{1},\mathbf{e}_{1})-\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\mathbf{g}},\mathbf{p}_{2},\mathbf{e}_{2})\|_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}+\mu_{1}}\left(\|\mathbf{p}_{1}-\mathbf{p}_{2}\|_{H^{2}(0,1)}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|\mathbf{p}_{1}-\mathbf{p}_{2}\|_{b}+\|\mathbf{e}_{1}-\mathbf{e}_{2}\|_{*}\right).

From fixed point theorem, we get (
8.41
) has a unique solution in
\mathcal{D}_{1}
. (
8.40
) follows from Lemma
8.3
and Lemma
8.4
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

With the help of Lemma
8.5
, we only need to consider the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{\mathbf{p}}=\mathcal{R}_{1}(\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}),\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})),\\
\tilde{\mathbf{e}}=\mathcal{R}_{2}(\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}),\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})).\end{array}\right.

Denote

\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=\left(\mathcal{R}_{1}(\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}),\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})),\mathcal{R}_{2}(\sigma\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{1\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}),\varepsilon\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{2\varepsilon}(\mathbf{h}_{k}+\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}}))\right).

From (
8.40
) and the fact
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{1\varepsilon}
, and
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}_{2\varepsilon}
are compact operators, we get
\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})
is a compact operator.

Consider the following problem via Schauder fixed point theorem

(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})=\mathcal{R}(\tilde{\mathbf{p}},\tilde{\mathbf{e}})

(8.42)

in

\mathcal{D}_{2}=\left\{(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{e})\;:\;\|\mathbf{p}\|_{b}\leq\frac{1}{|\log\varepsilon|^{\frac{3}{2}}},\;\|\mathbf{e}\|_{*}\leq\varepsilon^{1/2}\right\}.

We get (
8.42
) has a unique solution provided
\varepsilon>0
satisfying (
8.24
) and (
8.35
). Hence Theorem
1.2
follows.

\hfill\Box

Appendix A
Decay estimate of the solution to (
1.11
)

In this section, we assume the constant
p>1
and estimate the decay property of the function
w
.

Lemma A.1
.

The unique solution to (
1.11
) satisfies the following estimate

w(t)=\alpha_{p}e^{-\sqrt{p}|t|}+O(e^{-\min\{p,2\}\sqrt{p}|t|}),\qquad\mathrm{as}\qquad|t|\to\infty,

where the constant
\alpha_{p}>0
is of the following form:

\alpha_{p}=\frac{\sqrt{p}c_{p}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]\left(e^{\sqrt{p}t}-e^{-\sqrt{p}t}\right)w^{\prime}(t)dt.

Proof.

We use the method in
[
22
, Section 4]
to prove this lemma. It is well known that
w(t)
is an even function. We only need to consider the asymptotic behaviours of
w(t)
as
t\to\infty
. Let
g(s)=|s-1|^{p}-1+ps
. Then equation (
1.11
) is written into the following form

-w^{\prime\prime}+pw=g(w),\quad\mathrm{in}\quad\mathbb{R},\qquad w\to 0\quad\mathrm{as}\quad|t|\to\infty.

(A.1)

It is obvious that
g(w)=O(|w|^{\min\{p,2\}})
. For any constant
\varepsilon>0
, we find
r_{0}>0
large enough, such that
|g(w(t))|\leq\varepsilon w(t)
for
t>r_{0}
. Then we get

-w^{\prime\prime}+pw<\varepsilon w,\qquad\mathrm{for}\quad t>r_{0}.

(A.2)

Hence
w^{\prime\prime}>(p-\varepsilon)w>0
.
w^{\prime}
is an increasing function for
t>r_{0}
. Then we get
w^{\prime}(t)<0
for
t>r_{0}
.

Multiplying the both side of (
A.2
) by
2w^{\prime}
, we get

\left(\left(w^{\prime}\right)^{2}-(p-\varepsilon)w^{2}\right)^{\prime}<0,\quad\mathrm{for}\quad t>r_{0}.

Let
y(t)=\left(w^{\prime}\right)^{2}-(p-\varepsilon)w^{2}
. It is a decreasing function. Then
w^{\prime}(t)\to 0
as
t\to\infty
, for otherwise
y(t)\to c^{2}>0
as
t\to\infty
which implies that
w^{\prime}(t)\to-c
as
t\to+\infty
. It contradict with the fact that
w(t)\to 0
as
t\to\infty
. Hence
\left(w^{\prime}\right)^{2}-(p-\varepsilon)w^{2}\geq 0
for
t>r_{0}
. Then

w^{\prime}+\sqrt{p-\varepsilon}w\leq 0\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad t>r_{0}.

Hence

w(t)=O(e^{-a|t|})\qquad\mathrm{where}\qquad 0<a<\sqrt{p}.

(A.3)

Using Green function and (
A.1
), we get

w(t)=c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\sqrt{p}|t-s|}g(w(s))ds.

From (
A.3
), we get

e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}w(t)\leq c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}|s|}g(w(s))ds\leq C.

Hence
w(t)=O(e^{-\sqrt{p}|t|})
. Then

g(w(t))=O(e^{-\min\{p,2\}\sqrt{p}|t|}).

(A.4)

Now we consider the solution of the following problem

-u^{\prime\prime}+pu=f(t),\quad\mathrm{in}\quad\mathbb{R}.

(A.5)

Claim:
If the function
f(t)
satisfies the decay estimate
f(t)=O(e^{-\min\{p,2\}\sqrt{p}|t|})
as
|t|\to\infty
, we get

\lim_{t\to+\infty}e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}u(t)=c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}s}f(s)ds.

If
f
is a smooth function with compact support, we get

\lim_{t\to+\infty}e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}u(t)=\lim_{t\to+\infty}c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}(|t|-|t-s|)}f(s)ds=c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}s}f(s)ds.

However in the case of
f(t)=O(e^{-\min\{p,2\}\sqrt{p}|t|})
, we define the Banach space
B_{\tilde{\gamma}}
(
\sqrt{p}<\tilde{\gamma}<\min\{p,2\}\sqrt{p}
) with the norm

\|u\|_{\tilde{\gamma}}=\sup\{e^{\tilde{\gamma}|t|}|u(t)|\}.

Then
f\in B_{\tilde{\gamma}}
. There exists a sequence of smooth functions with compact supports
f_{n}\in C^{\infty}_{0}
such that
\|f_{n}-f\|_{\tilde{\gamma}}\to 0
. Denote the solution of (
A.5
) corresponding to
f=f_{n}
by
u_{n}
. Then we get

\displaystyle e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}|u(t)-u_{n}(t)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}(|t|-|t-s|)}|f(s)-f_{n}(s)|ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|f-f_{n}\|_{\tilde{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{(\sqrt{p}-\tilde{\gamma})|s|}ds\leq C\|f-f_{n}\|_{\tilde{\gamma}}.

Let
t\to+\infty
. Then we get

\left|\lim_{t\to+\infty}e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}u(t)-c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}s}f_{n}(s)ds\right|\leq C\|f-f_{n}\|_{\tilde{\gamma}}.

(A.6)

Using the dominated convergence theorem and (
A.6
), we get the claim.

From (
A.4
) and the claim, we get the solution
w
of (
A.1
) satisfies the estimate

\lim_{|t|\to\infty}e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}w(t)=\alpha_{p},

where the positive constant
\alpha_{p}
is of the following form

\displaystyle\alpha_{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{\sqrt{p}s}\left(|w-1|^{p}-1+pw\right)ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(e^{\sqrt{p}s}+e^{-\sqrt{p}s}\right)\left(|w-1|^{p}-1+pw\right)ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{p}c_{p}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right)\left(e^{\sqrt{p}s}+e^{-\sqrt{p}s}\right)w^{\prime}(s)ds.

Let

v(t)=w(t)-\alpha_{p}e^{-\sqrt{p}|t|}.

Then
v
satisfies

-v^{\prime\prime}+pv=g(w(t)),\quad\mathrm{for}\quad t\not=0,

and

\lim_{|t|\to\infty}e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}v(t)=0.

From L’Hôpital’s rule, we get

\lim_{|t|\to\infty}e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}v^{\prime}(t)=0.

Let
\tilde{w}(t)=e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}v(t)
. Then it satisfies

-\tilde{w}^{\prime\prime}+2\sqrt{p}\left(\mathrm{sgn}\;(t)\right)\tilde{w}^{\prime}=e^{\sqrt{p}|t|}g(w(t)),\quad\mathrm{for}\quad t\not=0,\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad w(\pm\infty)=w^{\prime}(\pm\infty)=0.

From theory in ordinary differential equation and (
A.4
)

\tilde{w}(t)=\int_{+\infty}^{t}e^{2\sqrt{p}x}dx\int_{+\infty}^{x}e^{-\sqrt{p}|s|}g(w(s))ds=O(e^{-\min\{p-1,1\}\sqrt{p}|t|}),

for
t>0
large enough. Hence this lemma follows.
∎

Appendix B
Property of the negative solutions of (
1.9
)

In this section, we consider the negative solutions of (
1.9
).

Proposition B.1
.

Assume that
\Omega
is a smooth bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{N}}(\mathcal{N}\geq 2)
. The constant
p\in(1,\frac{\mathcal{N}+2}{\mathcal{N}-2})
for
\mathcal{N}\geq 3
and
p>1
for
\mathcal{N}=1,2
. Let
\mathbf{\Psi}(x)
be an eigenfunction corresponding to the first Dirichlet eigenvalue of the operator
\mathfrak{L}(u)=-\Div(A(x)\nabla u)
on
\Omega
, where
A(x)=\{A_{ij}(x)\}_{2\times 2}
is a symmetric positive defined matrix function satisfying (
1.8
).
There exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, (
1.9
) has a unique negative solution
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}>-\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}
and the following estimate holds on any compact sets in
\Omega
:

\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}(x)=-\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}(x)-\varepsilon^{2}\left(\frac{\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})}{p\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x)}+o(1)\right),\quad\mathrm{as}\quad\varepsilon\to 0.

(B.1)

We will prove this Proposition
B.1
via a similar method in
[
32
, Theorem 1.1]
and
[
14
, Theorem 2.1]
.

Let
u=-w
. Problem (
1.9
) becomes following one:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla w)=\mathbf{\Psi}(x)-|w|^{p},&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
w=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$}.\end{array}\right.

(B.2)

Consider the following problem first

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla\tilde{w})=h(x,\tilde{w}),&\mbox{in $\Omega$,}\\
\tilde{w}=0,&\mbox{on $\partial\Omega$},\end{array}\right.

(B.3)

where

h(x,t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbf{\Psi}(x),&\mbox{for $t<0$,}\\
\mathbf{\Psi}(x)-|t|^{p},&\mbox{for $t\geq 0$.}\end{array}\right.

The energy functional of (
B.3
) is

J_{\varepsilon}(\tilde{w})=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{\Omega}\langle A(x)\nabla\tilde{w},\nabla\tilde{w}\rangle dx-\int_{\Omega}H(x,\tilde{w})dx,\qquad\tilde{w}\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega),

where
H(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}h(x,\tau)d\tau
.
The functional
J_{\varepsilon}
is bounded from below on
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
. Let
\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}
be its minimizer.

From the definition of
h(x,t)
, we get
H(x,t)<0
for
t<0
or
t>M_{3}
, where
M_{3}
is a large positive constant. Then
0<\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}<M_{3}
and
\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}
solves (
B.3
).

From direct computation,
0
is a subsolution of (
B.3
) whereas
\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}
is a supersolution of (
B.3
). From the same argument of
[
10
, Lemma A.1]
, we find a solution
\tilde{w}
of (
B.3
) satisfies
0\leq\tilde{w}(x)\leq\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}
. Hence
\tilde{w}
solves (
B.2
).

A direct computation yields that the positive solution of (
B.2
) is unique. Hence
\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}=\tilde{w}
. Then

0\leq\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}(x)\leq\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(B.4)

To study the asymptotic behaviors of
\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}
, we consider the following problem

\inf\{I(u)\;:\;u-c\in H^{1}_{0}(B_{1}(0))\},

(B.5)

where
c\geq 0
is a constant,

I(u)=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{1}(0)}\langle A(x)\nabla u,\nabla u\rangle dx-\int_{B_{1}(0)}\bar{H}(u)dx,\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\bar{H}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\bar{h}(\tau)d\tau.

Here
\bar{h}(t)
is a non-increasing function satisfies
\bar{h}(t)>0
for
t\in(-\infty,a)
and
\bar{h}(t)\leq 0
for
t>a
, where
a\geq 0
is a constant.

Lemma B.2
.

If
a\geq c
and
u_{\varepsilon}
is the minimizer of problem (
B.5
),
u_{\varepsilon}(x)\to a
on compact sets in
B_{1}(0)
, as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Proof.

To prove this lemma, we only need to modify the argument in
[
32
, Lemma 2.2]
. Since
\bar{H}(t)<0
for
t<0
, we get
u_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
. Otherwise
u_{\varepsilon}^{+}=\max\{u_{\varepsilon},0\}
has less energy.

It is apparent that
u_{\varepsilon}
is a solution of the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla u_{\varepsilon})=\bar{h}(u_{\varepsilon}),&\mbox{in $B_{1}(0)$,}\\
u_{\varepsilon}=c,&\mbox{on $\partial B_{1}(0)$.}\end{array}\right.

(B.6)

Multiplying the both side of (
B.6
) by
(u_{\varepsilon}-a)_{+}
and integrating by part, we get
u_{\varepsilon}\leq a
. Hence
0\leq u_{\varepsilon}\leq a
and
\bar{h}(u_{\varepsilon})\geq 0
. From elliptic estimate, we get
u_{\varepsilon}\in C^{1,\gamma}(B_{1}(0))
for
\gamma\in(0,1)
.

Using the same method as above, (
B.6
) and the non-increasing property of
\bar{h}
, we get

u_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x)\leq u_{\varepsilon_{2}}(x)\quad\mathrm{for}\quad 0<\varepsilon_{2}<\varepsilon_{1},\quad\mathrm{and}\quad x\in B_{1}(0).

(B.7)

For any fixed point
x_{0}\in B_{1}(0)
, we define
\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(x)=u_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}+\varepsilon x)
. It solves the following equation

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\Div(A(x_{0}+\varepsilon x)\nabla\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon})=\bar{h}(\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon}),&\mbox{in $D_{1}$,}\\
\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(x)=c,&\mbox{on $\partial D_{1}$.}\end{array}\right.

where
D_{1}=\left(B_{1}(0)-x_{0}\right)/\varepsilon
. From elliptic estimate, we get
\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon}
converges to a
C^{1,\gamma}
function
\tilde{v}
on compact sets. The function
\tilde{v}(x)
satisfies

-\Div(A(x_{0})\nabla\tilde{v})=\bar{h}(\tilde{v}),\qquad\mathrm{in}\qquad\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(B.8)

For any constant
b>1
,
\varepsilon/b<\varepsilon
. From (
B.7
), we get

\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(x)=u_{\varepsilon}(x_{0}+\varepsilon x)\leq u_{\varepsilon/b}(x_{0}+\varepsilon x)=\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon/b}(bx).

Let
\varepsilon\to 0
. Then we have
\tilde{v}(x)\leq\tilde{v}(bx)
. Hence the minimum of
\tilde{v}
is attained at
0
. From maximum principal, we get
\tilde{v}
is a constant function.
From (
B.8
), we get
\tilde{v}(x)\equiv a
. Then
u_{\varepsilon}(x_{0})=\tilde{v}_{\varepsilon}(0)\to a
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Hence
u_{\varepsilon}(x)\to a
as
\varepsilon\to 0
for any
x\in B_{1}(0)
.

For any compact set
K\subset B_{1}(0)
, we have

\min_{\partial K}u_{\varepsilon}\leq u_{\varepsilon}(x)\leq a,\qquad\mathrm{for}\qquad\forall x\in K,

(B.9)

from maximum principal.

Now we prove
\min_{\partial K}u_{\varepsilon}\to a
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. There is
x_{\varepsilon}\in\partial K
such that
u_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})=\min_{\partial K}u_{\varepsilon}
. Hence
x_{\varepsilon}\to z_{0}\in\partial K
. Then for
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{1}
, we have
u_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x_{\varepsilon})\leq u_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})
. Hence

u_{\varepsilon_{1}}(z_{0})=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}u_{\varepsilon_{1}}(x_{\varepsilon})\leq\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}u_{\varepsilon}(x_{\varepsilon})\leq a.

Let
\varepsilon_{1}\to 0
, we get this fact.

From (
B.9
), we know this lemma holds.
∎

Now we consider the following minimization problem

\inf\left\{\bar{J}_{\varepsilon}(u,D)=\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{D}\langle A(x)\nabla u,\nabla u\rangle-\int_{D}\bar{G}(x,u)\;:\;u-\eta\in H^{1}_{0}(D)\right\},

(B.10)

where
D\subset\Omega
,
\eta\in H^{1}(D)
and
\bar{G}(x,t)=\int_{0}^{t}\bar{g}(x,\tau)d\tau
. By repeating the argument in
[
13
, Lemma 2.3]
, we get

Lemma B.3
.

If
u_{\varepsilon i}
is the minimizer of (
B.10
) corresponding to
\bar{g}=g_{i}
and
\eta=\eta_{i}
,
i=1,2
. If
\eta_{1}\geq\eta_{2}
and

g_{1}(x,t)\geq g_{2}(x,t),\quad\mathrm{for}\quad x\in D\quad\mathrm{and}\quad t\in\left[\min_{i=1,2}\min_{z\in D}u_{\varepsilon i}(z),\max_{i=1,2}\max_{z\in D}u_{\varepsilon i}(z)\right],

Then
u_{\varepsilon 1}\geq u_{\varepsilon 2}
in
D
.

With the help of Lemma
B.2
, Lemma
B.3
and (
B.4
), we get
\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}
converges to
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}
on compact sets in
\Omega
from the same argument
[
32
, Lemma 2.3]
.

Notice that the function
\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}
is a positive continuous function on
\Omega
and

f(x):=\frac{\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})}{p\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}

is a negative valued continuous function.

Fixed any
x_{0}\in\Omega
. For any
\eta>0
sufficient small, there exist
\eta_{1}>0
satisfying
(-f(x_{0})+\eta)\eta_{1}<\eta
. So we find a constant
\delta>0
such that
B(x_{0})\subset\Omega
and for
|x-x_{0}|<\delta
, the following inequalities hold:

\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x)>\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x_{0}),\quad|f(x)-f(x_{0})|<\eta,\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\left|\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x)-\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x_{0})\right|<\frac{1}{4}\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x_{0})\eta_{1}.

Notice
\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}
is the minimizer of the functional
J_{\varepsilon}
on
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
. we get

\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\langle A(x)\nabla\underline{u}_{\varepsilon},\nabla\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}\rangle dx-\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}H(x,\underline{u}_{\varepsilon})dx\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\langle A(x)\nabla\bar{w},\nabla\bar{w}\rangle dx-\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}H(x,\bar{w})dx,

where
\bar{w}-\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}\in H_{0}^{1}(B_{\delta}(x_{0}))
. Let
v_{\varepsilon}=\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}-\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}
. Then
-M_{3}\leq-(\mathbf{\Psi}(x))^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq v_{\varepsilon}(x)\leq 0
and

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\langle A(x)\nabla v_{\varepsilon},\nabla v_{\varepsilon}\rangle dx-\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\left[H(x,v_{\varepsilon}+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})+\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})v_{\varepsilon}\right]dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\langle A(x)\nabla\bar{w},\nabla\bar{w}\rangle dx-\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\left[H(x,\bar{w}+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})+\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})\bar{w}\right]dx,

where
\bar{w}-v_{\varepsilon}\in H_{0}^{1}(B_{\delta}(x_{0}))
. Hence
v_{\varepsilon}
is the minimizer of the following problem

\inf\left\{\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\langle A(x)\nabla\bar{w},\nabla\bar{w}\rangle dx-\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}G(x,\bar{w})dx;\;\;\bar{w}-v_{\varepsilon}\in H_{0}^{1}(B_{\delta}(x_{0}))\right\},

(B.11)

where

G(x,t)=H(x,t+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})+\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})t.

It is apparent that

h(x,t+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathbf{\Psi}(x)-|t+\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}|^{p},&t>-\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}},\\
\mathbf{\Psi}(x),&t\leq-\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}.\end{array}\right.

It is easy to see that for
t>-\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}
, we get

\mathbf{\Psi}(x)-\left|t+\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right|^{p}\leq-p\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t,

and

\mathbf{\Psi}(x)-\left|t+\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right|^{p}\geq-p\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t-M_{4}t^{2}-M_{5}|t|^{p},

where
M_{4}
and
M_{5}
are positive constant large enough.
Especially, for
-\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}<t<0
, we have

\mathbf{\Psi}(x)-\left|t+\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right|^{p}\geq-p\mathbf{\Psi}(x)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t-\tilde{M}|t|^{\min\{2,p\}}.

Let
\tilde{f}(t)=-pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t-\tilde{M}|t|^{\min\{2,p\}}
and
t_{0}
is the largest negative maximum point of
\tilde{f}(t)
. We define

h_{1}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}pB,&\mbox{if $t<-B^{\frac{1}{p}}$},\\
-pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t,&\mbox{if $-B^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq t<0$},\\
-pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t,&\mbox{if $t\geq 0$},\end{array}\right.

and

h_{2}(t)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\tilde{f}(t_{0}),&\mbox{if $t<t_{0}$},\\
\tilde{f}(t),&\mbox{if $t_{0}\leq t<0$},\\
-pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}t-M_{4}t^{2}-M_{5}|t|^{p},&\mbox{if $t\geq 0$},\end{array}\right.

where

B=\sup_{x\in B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\mathbf{\Psi}(x),\quad\mathrm{and}\quad b=\inf_{x\in B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\mathbf{\Psi}(x).

It is apparent that

h_{2}(t)\leq h(x,t+\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}(x))\leq h_{1}(t).

(B.12)

Then for any
x,y\in B_{\delta}(x_{0})
, we get

\left|\frac{\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(x)}{\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(y)}-1\right|<\eta_{1},

and

X_{1}<\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})<Y_{1},

(B.13)

where
X_{1}=-pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\eta+pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}f(x_{0})
,
Y_{1}=pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\eta+pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}f(x_{0})
.

In order to estimate
v_{\varepsilon}
which is the minimizer of (
B.11
), we study the following problem

\min\left\{\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2}\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}\langle A(x)\nabla w,\nabla w\rangle dx-\int_{B_{\delta}(x_{0})}H_{i}(w)+\varepsilon^{2}a_{0}w\;:\;w-c_{0}\in H_{0}^{1}(B_{\delta}(x_{0}))\right\},

(B.14)

where
i=1,2
,
c_{0}\leq 0
,
a_{0}\leq 0
and
H_{i}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}h_{i}(\tau)d\tau
. Let
w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}
be the minimizer of (
B.14
) and it solves the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-\varepsilon^{2}\Div(A(x)\nabla w)=\tilde{g}_{i}(w),&\mbox{in $B_{\delta}(x_{0})$},\\
w=c_{0},&\mbox{on $\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})$},\end{array}\right.

where
\tilde{g}_{i}(t):=h_{i}(t)+\varepsilon^{2}a_{0}
. It is apparent that
\tilde{g}_{i}(t)
has a falling zero which we denote by
t_{i,\varepsilon}
. In fact

t_{1,\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{2}\frac{a_{0}}{pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}},\quad\mathrm{and}\quad t_{2,\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{2}\frac{a_{0}}{pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}+o(\varepsilon^{2}).

From the similar method as in Lemma
B.2
, we get
w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}
converges to
0
uniformly on compact sets in
\Omega
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. We also have
c_{0}<w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}<t_{i,\varepsilon}
for
c_{0}<0
and
t_{i,\varepsilon}<w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}<0
for
c_{0}=0
. We only consider the case
c_{0}<0
for simplicity since similar conclusions below also hold in the case
c_{0}=0
via the same method.

Let
\psi_{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\log(t_{i,\varepsilon}-w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}})
. Then it solves the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\varepsilon\Div(A(x)\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon})-\langle A(x)\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon},\nabla\psi_{\varepsilon}\rangle+e^{\psi_{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon}g(t_{i,\varepsilon}-e^{-\psi_{\varepsilon}/\varepsilon})=0,&\mbox{in $B_{\delta}(x_{0})$}\\
\psi_{\varepsilon}=-\varepsilon\log(t_{i,\varepsilon}-c_{0}),&\mbox{on $\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})$}.\end{array}\right.

(B.15)

In order to estimate the solutions of (
B.15
), we consider the following problem as in
[
36
, Lemma 4.2]
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\varepsilon\Div(A(x)\nabla\psi)-\langle A(x)\nabla\psi,\nabla\psi\rangle+1=0,&\mbox{in $B_{\delta}(x_{0})$}\\
\psi=0,&\mbox{on $\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})$}.\end{array}\right.

(B.16)

Lemma B.4
.

For
\varepsilon>0
small enough, (
B.16
) has a unique solution
\psi^{\varepsilon}
. There’s a constant
C_{1}>0
, such that
\|\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{1}
. Moreover, it has the following estimate

\mu d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0}))\leq\psi^{\varepsilon}(x)\leq\rho d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})),

(B.17)

where
\mu
and
\rho
are positive constants.

Proof.

It is obvious that
0
is a subsolution of (
B.16
). With the help of (
1.8
), we fix a vector
X_{0}
such that
\langle A(x)X_{0},X_{0}\rangle>2
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Choose positive constant
b
large enough, such that
g(x)=\langle x,X_{0}\rangle+b>0
on
\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})
. For
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we get

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\varepsilon\Div(A(x)\nabla g)-\langle A(x)\nabla g,\nabla g\rangle+1<0,&\mbox{in $B_{\delta}(x_{0})$},\\
g>0,&\mbox{on $\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})$}.\end{array}\right.

From
[
1
, Theorem 1]
, we get a solution
\psi^{\varepsilon}
of (
B.16
) satisfying
0<\psi^{\varepsilon}<g(x)
. Moreover, the solution of (
B.16
) is unique via maximum principal. Then we have
\|\psi^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{\delta}(x_{0}))}\leq C_{1}
.

Now we prove the estimate (
B.17
). Let
d(x)=d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0}))
. For
x\not=x_{0}
,
d(x)
is a
C^{2}
function. We define
\psi_{\varepsilon}^{+}(x)=\rho d(x)
where
\rho
is a positive constant large enough, so that
\psi_{\varepsilon}^{+}(x)
is a supersolution of (
B.16
).

Let
\psi_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x)=\mu d(x)
, where
\mu
is a positive small enough so that
\psi_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x)
is a subsolution of (
B.16
). Hence the estimate (
B.17
) holds.
∎

From the similar argument of
[
12
, Theorem 2.1]
, we get an estimate of (
B.15
).

Lemma B.5
.

The solution
\psi_{\varepsilon}
of (
B.15
) has the following estimate

\mu\nu_{0}d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0}))\leq\psi_{\varepsilon}(x)\leq\rho\nu_{0}d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})),

where
\nu_{0}=\sqrt{-\tilde{g}_{i}^{\prime}(t_{i,\varepsilon})}
.

Proof.

Let
\bar{\delta}\in(0,\delta)
be a constant sufficient near
\delta
. Then
t_{i,\varepsilon}-w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}
converges to
0
on
B_{\bar{\delta}}(x_{0})
. For any
\eta>0
, we have
t_{i,\varepsilon}-w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}<\eta
on
B_{\bar{\delta}}(x_{0})
for small
\varepsilon
. Let
w_{\varepsilon}^{+}
be the unique solution of the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\varepsilon\Div(A(x)\nabla w_{\varepsilon}^{+})-\langle A(x)\nabla w_{\varepsilon}^{+},\nabla w_{\varepsilon}^{+}\rangle+\tau=0,&\mbox{in $B_{\bar{\delta}}(x_{0})$},\\
w_{\varepsilon}^{+}=0,&\mbox{on $\partial B_{\bar{\delta}}(x_{0})$},\end{array}\right.

(B.18)

where

\tau=\min_{t_{i,\varepsilon}-\eta<s<t_{i,\varepsilon}}(-\tilde{g}_{i}^{\prime}(s)),\qquad\tilde{\tau}=\max_{t_{i,\varepsilon}-\eta<s<t_{i,\varepsilon}}(-\tilde{g}_{i}^{\prime}(s)).

It is apparent that
\psi_{\varepsilon}
is a supersolution of (
B.18
). Then we have

\psi_{\varepsilon}(x)\geq w_{\varepsilon}^{+}(x)\geq\sqrt{\tau}\mu d(x,\partial B_{\bar{\delta}}(x_{0})),\quad\mathrm{where}\quad x\in B_{\bar{\delta}}(x_{0})).

(B.19)

Now we construct a supersolution of (
B.15
). Define

T=\{x\in B_{\delta}(x_{0}):w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}>t_{i,\varepsilon}-\eta\}.

Since
t_{i,\varepsilon}-w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}
converges to
0
on compact sets, we realize that given any compact set
K\subset B_{\delta}(x_{0})
, we have
K\subset T
for
\varepsilon
small enough. Let
w_{\varepsilon}^{-}
be the unique solution of the following problem

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\varepsilon\Div(A(x)\nabla w_{\varepsilon}^{-})-\langle A(x)\nabla w_{\varepsilon}^{-},\nabla w_{\varepsilon}^{-}\rangle+\tilde{\tau}=0,&\mbox{in $B_{\delta}(x_{0})$},\\
w_{\varepsilon}^{-}=\tilde{e},&\mbox{on $\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0})$},\end{array}\right.

where
\tilde{e}
is a fixed constant some enough. From Lemma
B.4
, we get

w_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x)\geq\sqrt{\tilde{\tau}}\mu d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0}))+\tilde{e}.

It is easy to see that
w_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x)
is a supersolution of (
B.15
) on
T
. However, on
\partial T
, we have
\psi_{\varepsilon}(x)=-\varepsilon\log\eta\leq\tilde{e}/2\leq w_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x)
. Hence the following estimate holds on
T
:

\psi_{\varepsilon}(x)\leq w_{\varepsilon}^{-}(x)\leq\sqrt{\tilde{\tau}}\rho d(x,\partial B_{\delta}(x_{0}))+\tilde{e}.

(B.20)

On
B_{\delta}(x_{0})\char 92\relax T
, we also get the estimate above from Lemma
B.4
and direct computation.

Let
\bar{\delta}\to\delta
,
\eta\to 0
and
\tilde{e}\to 0
, we get this lemma from (
B.19
) and (
B.20
).
∎

With the help of Lemma
B.5
, we get that we get
w^{\varepsilon}_{i,a_{0},c_{0}}=t_{i}+o(\varepsilon^{2})
in
B_{\frac{\delta}{2}}(x_{0})
. That is

w^{\varepsilon}_{1,a_{0},c_{0}}=\varepsilon^{2}\frac{a_{0}}{pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}+o(\varepsilon^{2}),\quad\mathrm{and}\quad w^{\varepsilon}_{2,a_{0},c_{0}}=\varepsilon^{2}\frac{a_{0}}{pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}+o(\varepsilon^{2}).

From (
B.12
), (
B.13
) and Lemma
B.3
, we also get

w^{\varepsilon}_{2,X_{1},-M}\leq v_{\varepsilon}\leq w^{\varepsilon}_{1,Y_{1},0}\quad\mathrm{in}\quad B_{\delta}(x_{0}).

For
\varepsilon>0
small enough, we get

\frac{X_{1}}{pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}-\eta\leq\frac{w^{\varepsilon}_{1,X_{1},-M}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{v_{\varepsilon}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{w^{\varepsilon}_{2,Y_{1},0}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leq\frac{Y_{1}}{pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}+\eta.

In fact,

\frac{Y_{1}}{pB^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}+\eta\leq f(x_{0})+2\eta-f(x_{0})\eta_{1}\leq f(x)+4\eta,

and

\frac{X_{1}}{pb^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}-\eta\geq f(x_{0})-2\eta+(f(x_{0})-\eta)\eta_{1}\geq f(x)-4\eta.

Hence we get

\left|\frac{v_{\varepsilon}(x)}{\varepsilon^{2}}-f(x)\right|\leq 4\eta,\quad\mathrm{where}\quad x\in B_{\frac{\delta}{2}}(x_{0}).

Let
K
be a compact subset in
\Omega
. For any
\eta>0
, we cover
K
by a finite number of balls
B_{\frac{\delta}{2}}(x_{0})
,
x_{0}\in K
. Using the relationship above, we get for
\varepsilon
small enough,

\left|\frac{v_{\varepsilon}(x)}{\varepsilon^{2}}-f(x)\right|\leq 4\eta,\quad\mathrm{where}\quad x\in K.

Then we get

\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}(x)=\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}}(x)+\varepsilon^{2}\left(\frac{\Div(A(x)\nabla\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{1}{p}})}{p\mathbf{\Psi}^{\frac{p-1}{p}}}+o(1)\right),\qquad x\in K.

Then
\bar{u}_{\varepsilon}:=\underline{u}_{\varepsilon}
is the unique negative solution of (
1.9
) satisfying (
B.1
). Hence Proposition
B.1
follows.

Appendix C
Projections of the error

Recall
S(\mathcal{V})
is expanded in (
3.28
). In this section, we expand the terms

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})w_{j,x}dx\qquad\mathrm{and}\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})w_{j,x}dx.

We first consider the term
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})w_{j,x}dx
. It is easy to get

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\varepsilon(\lambda_{0}e_{k}+\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime})Z_{k}w_{j,x}=O(\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}\sum_{k\not=j}(|e_{k}|+\varepsilon^{2}|e_{k}^{\prime\prime}|)).

From (
3.24
), we get

\sum_{k\not=j}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{k}(z,x-\beta f_{k})w_{j,x}dx=O(\varepsilon^{3})\sum_{k\not=j}[|f_{k}|+|f_{k}^{\prime}|+|f_{k}^{\prime\prime}|+\varepsilon|e_{k}^{\prime}|(|f_{k}|+|f_{k}^{\prime}|)].

However

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}p\alpha_{p}\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}[|w_{j}-1|^{p-2}(w_{j}-1)+1]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{j+1})}\right)w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}^{\frac{\beta}{2}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}p\alpha_{p}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}x}e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}x}e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]w_{x}(x)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\alpha_{p}C_{0}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]+O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}),

where

C_{0}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1](e^{-\sqrt{p}x}-e^{\sqrt{p}x})w_{x}dx>0

from Lemma
A.1
.
Along the same lines, we get

\sum_{k\not=j}p\alpha_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{k}}[|w_{k}-1|^{p-2}(w_{k}-1)+1]\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{k-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{k+1})}\right]w_{j,x}dx=O(\varepsilon^{\frac{5}{2}+\mu_{1}}),

We first derive some identities. Taking derivatives of both sides of (
3.18
), we get

\displaystyle-w_{0,xxx}-p|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)w_{0,x}-p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{x}

(C.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{xx}+\frac{2p}{p+3}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]+\frac{2p(p-1)}{p+3}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{x}.

Multiplying the both side of (
C.1
) by
w_{2}
and
w_{3}
, respectively and integrating, we get the following identities from (
3.20
) and (
3.21
):

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}[w_{0,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{2}]w_{x}dx

(C.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{2,x}w_{x}dx-\frac{2p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]w_{2}dx-\frac{2p(p-1)}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{2}w_{x}dx

and

\displaystyle(p-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{x}dx+p(p-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{3}w_{x}dx

(C.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{3,x}w_{x}dx-\frac{2p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]w_{3}dx-\frac{2p(p-1)}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}w_{x}dx.

From (
3.19
), (
3.20
) and (
3.21
), we get the following identities from the same method as above:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}[w_{1,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{2}]w_{x}dx

(C.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{2,x}w_{x}dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}xw_{2,xx}w_{x}dx+\frac{p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]w_{2}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{p(p-1)}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{2}w_{x}dx,

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}[xw_{3,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{3}]w_{x}dx+(p-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{x}dx

(C.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{3,x}w_{x}dx+\frac{p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]w_{3}dx+\frac{p(p-1)}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}w_{x}dx.

From the condition (
2.10
) and direct computation, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{j}(z,x-\beta f_{j})w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx\left\{-a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime\prime}-2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}+a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}\right.

\displaystyle-b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}+2a_{11}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}f_{j}-a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{-1}(\beta^{\prime})^{2}f_{j}+a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime\prime}f_{j}+2a_{22}\alpha^{-p}\alpha^{\prime}\beta f_{j}

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{p+3}{2}\beta^{-1}\alpha^{-1}\mathbf{q}_{tt}f_{j}-a_{33}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{j}-2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{j}+b_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{j}+b_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{j}+2a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{j}\right\}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx\left\{-2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}(\beta f_{j})^{\prime}e_{j}+2\varepsilon a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{\prime}f_{j}e_{j}^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}\alpha^{1-p}\beta f_{j}e_{j}^{\prime}\right\}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}(a_{32})^{2}f_{j}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[w_{1,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{2}+xw_{2,xx}-\frac{p(p-1)}{p+3}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{2}\right]w_{x}dx\right.

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[xw_{3,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{3}-\frac{p(p-1)}{p+3}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}+(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}\right]w_{x}dx

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{p(p-1)}{(p+3)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}-1\right]w_{x}dx\right\}

\displaystyle\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}a_{32}b_{21}f_{j}\left\{\left[w_{0,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{2}+w_{2,x}+\frac{2p(p-1)}{p+3}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{2}\right]w_{x}dx\right.

\displaystyle-\frac{p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{3}+(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}+\frac{2p(p-1)}{p+3}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}+w_{3,x}\right]w_{x}dx

\displaystyle+\frac{2p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[xw_{3,xx}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}w_{3}+(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{1}-\frac{p(p-1)}{p+3}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}\right]w_{x}dx

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{4p(p-1)}{(p+3)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}-1\right]w_{x}dx\right\}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}(b_{21})^{2}f_{j}\left\{\frac{2p}{p+3}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}+p(p-1)|w-1|^{p-2}w_{0}w_{3}+w_{3,x}\right]w_{x}dx\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{4p^{2}(p-1)}{(p+3)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|w-1|^{p-2}xw_{3}w_{x}dx+\frac{4p(p-1)}{(p+3)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}-1\right]w_{x}dx\right\}.

From (
3.15
), (
3.16
) and direct computation, we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{2,x}w_{x}dx=-\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{3,x}w_{x}dx=-\frac{p+3}{4p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]w_{2}dx=\frac{p+3}{4p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}x\left[|w-1|^{p-2}-1\right]w_{x}dx=-\frac{1}{p-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]dx+\frac{p+3}{2p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx,

and

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]w_{3}dx=\frac{(p+3)(p-3)}{4p^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx-\frac{1}{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]dx.

From these identities, (
2.10
), (
3.15
), (
3.16
), (
C.2
), (
C.3
), (
C.4
) and (
C.5
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{j}(z,x-\beta f_{j})w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha^{1-p}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx\left\{-a_{11}f_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\left[a_{22}-b_{11}-a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)\right]f_{j}^{\prime}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left[a_{22}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)+b_{22}-a_{33}+\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}\right]f_{j}\right\}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{1-p}\beta\left(-2\varepsilon a_{11}e_{j}^{\prime}f_{j}^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}e_{j}^{\prime}f_{j}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx

\displaystyle-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}a_{32}b_{21}f_{j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx+\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}(a_{32})^{2}f_{j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{2-2p}\beta^{3}(b_{21})^{2}f_{j}\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx.

However, from the definitions of
\tilde{\theta}_{j}
and
\tilde{\theta}_{j2}
in (
3.12
) and (
3.29
), we get

\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\theta}_{k}w_{j,x}=O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}),\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\theta}_{k2}w_{j,x}=O(\varepsilon^{3-\mu})

In summary, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})w_{j,x}dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\alpha^{1-p}\beta\int_{\mathbb{R}}w_{x}^{2}dx\left\{-a_{11}f_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\left[a_{22}-b_{11}-a_{11}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)\right]f_{j}^{\prime}\right.

(C.6)

\displaystyle+\left[a_{22}\left(\beta^{-1}\beta^{\prime}+2\alpha^{-1}\alpha^{\prime}\right)+b_{22}-a_{33}+\frac{p+3}{2}\alpha^{p-2}\beta^{-2}\mathbf{q}_{tt}\right]f_{j}

\displaystyle\left.+\left[\frac{p+2}{2(p+3)}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(a_{32})^{2}-\frac{p+1}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}a_{32}b_{21}-\frac{2}{p+3}\alpha^{1-p}\beta^{2}(b_{21})^{2}\right]f_{j}\right\}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\alpha^{1-p}\beta\left(-2\varepsilon a_{11}e_{j}^{\prime}f_{j}^{\prime}+2\varepsilon a_{22}e_{j}^{\prime}f_{j}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{x}w_{x}dx

\displaystyle+p\alpha_{p}C_{0}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}-e^{-\sqrt{p}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]+\varepsilon^{2}\Theta_{j}(\varepsilon z)

where
\|\Theta_{j}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\mu}
.

Now we consider the term
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})Z_{j}(x)dx
. It is easy to get

\sum_{k=1}^{N}\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{k}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{k})\int_{\mathbb{R}}Z_{k}Z_{j}dx=\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{j})+O\left(\varepsilon^{3-\mu}\sum_{k\not=j}\left(|e_{k}|+|e_{k}^{\prime\prime}|\right)\right)

and

\sum_{k=1}^{N}\varepsilon^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{k}(z,x-\beta f_{k})Z_{j}(x)dx=O\left(\varepsilon^{4-\mu}\sum_{k\not=j}\left[|f_{k}|+|f_{k}^{\prime}|+|f_{k}^{\prime\prime}|+\varepsilon|e_{k}^{\prime}|\left(|f_{k}|+|f_{k}^{\prime}|\right)\right]\right).

However,

\displaystyle p\alpha_{p}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\chi_{\mathcal{U}_{j}}\left[|w_{k}-1|^{p-2}(w_{k}-1)\right]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{k-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{k+1})}\right)Z_{j}(x)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\alpha_{p}\int_{\frac{1}{2}\beta(f_{j-1}+f_{j})}^{\frac{1}{2}\beta(f_{j+1}+f_{j})}\left[|w_{k}-1|^{p-2}(w_{k}-1)\right]\left(e^{-\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{k-1})}+e^{\sqrt{p}(x-\beta f_{k+1})}\right)Z_{j}(x)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\alpha_{p}e^{-\sqrt{p}(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}\int_{\frac{1}{2}\beta(f_{j-1}-f_{j})}^{\frac{1}{2}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]e^{-\sqrt{p}x}Z(x)dx

\displaystyle+p\alpha_{p}e^{-\sqrt{p}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\int_{\frac{1}{2}\beta(f_{j-1}-f_{j})}^{\frac{1}{2}\beta(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\left[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1\right]e^{\sqrt{p}x}Z(x)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p\alpha_{p}C_{1}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}+e^{-\sqrt{p}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right],

where

C_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}[|w-1|^{p-2}(w-1)+1]\left(e^{\sqrt{p}x}+e^{-\sqrt{p}x}\right)Z(x)dx.

Other terms is estimated by a similar method. We get

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\varepsilon}S(\mathcal{V})Z_{j}(x)dx=\varepsilon(\varepsilon^{2}a_{11}\alpha^{1-p}e_{j}^{\prime\prime}+\lambda_{0}e_{j})+p\alpha_{p}C_{1}\left[e^{-\sqrt{p}(f_{j}-f_{j-1})}+e^{-\sqrt{p}(f_{j+1}-f_{j})}\right]+\varepsilon^{2}\Xi_{j}(\varepsilon z),

(C.7)

where
\|\Xi_{j}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\mu}
.

No data was used for the research described in the article.
